nammāḻvār aruḷiya tiruvāymoḻi (pākam 2) kucalyā hārṭ avarkaḷiṉ āṅkila moḻipeyarppuṭaṉ Divyaprabandham - Thiruvaymozhi - Aaraam Pathu 3343 - 3452.

tirucciṟṟampalam
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (mutal tirumoḻi, 3343-3353)
tiruvaṇvaṇṭūrp perumāṉiṭam talivi paṟavikaḷit tūtuviṭal (tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr)
3343 vikal pūṅ kaḻivāy vantu mēyum kurukiṉaṅkāḷ
cey koḷ cennel uyar tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr uṟiyum
ki koḷ cakkarattu eṉ kaṉivāyp perumāṉik kaṇṭu
kikaḷ kūppi collīr viṉiyāṭṭiyēṉ kātaṉmiyē (1)
3343. She says, "O kurugu birds,
you come in the morning every day
and wander in the flourishing backwaters.
Go and see the lord whose mouth is as sweet as a fruit.
He carries a discus and stays in Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
where the good paddy grows tall.
Fold your hands and worship him
and tell him that I suffer from love for him.
I have done bad karma."
3344 kātal meṉ peṭiyōṭu uṭaṉ mēyum karu nārāy
vēta vēḷvi oli muḻaṅkum taṇ tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr
nātaṉ ñālam ellām uṇṭa nam perumāṉik kaṇṭu
pātam kitoḻutu paṇiyīr aṭiyēṉ tiṟamē (2)
3344. She says, "O large cranes,
you wander here with your beloved mates.
He swallowed all the worlds.
Go and see our dear lord.
He is the lord of cool Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
where the Vedas are recited loudly
when the Brahmins perform sacrifices.
Bow to his feet and tell him how I suffer from his love."
3345 tiṟaṅkaḷ āki eṅkum ceykaḷ ūṭu uḻal puḷḷiṉaṅkāḷ
ciṟanta celvam malku tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr uṟiyum
kaṟaṅku cakkarak kik kaṉivāyp perumāṉik kaṇṭu
iṟaṅki nīr toḻutu paṇiyīr aṭiyēṉ iṭarē (3)
3345. She says, "O flock of birds,
you all come in a crowd
and fly around everywhere on these fields.
Go and see the dear god with a whirling discus in his hand.
His mouth is as sweet as a fruit
and he is the lord of beautiful Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
flourishing with wealth.
Fly down to the ground, bow to him
and tell him how I suffer with love for him.
I am his slave."
3346 iṭar il pōkam mūḻki iṇintu āṭum maṭa aṉṉaṅkāḷ
viṭal il vēta oli muḻaṅkum taṇ tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr
kaṭaliṉ mēṉippirāṉ kaṇṇaṉi neṭumālik kaṇṭu
uṭalam nintu orutti urukum eṉṟu uṇarttumiṉē (4)
3346. She says, "O lovely swans, you join your mates
and plunge into happiness without any worries.
Go and see the god Neḍumaal, the ocean-colored Kaṇṇan.
He is the god of flourishing Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
where the sound of the Vedas spreads everywhere.
Make him understand that there is a girl
who suffers and melts with love for him."
3347 uṇarttal ūṭal uṇarntu uṭaṉ mēyum maṭa aṉṉaṅkāḷ
tiṇartta vaṇṭalkaḷmēl caṅku cērum tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr
puṇartta pūn taṇ tuḻāy muṭi nam perumāṉik kaṇṭu
puṇartta kiyiṉarāy aṭiyēṉukkum pōṟṟumiṉē (5)
3347. She says, "O lovely swans,
you know how it is to fight and make up with your mates
and you wander together happily without being apart from them.
Go and see our dear lord
whose head is adorned with a cool beautiful thulasi garland.
He stays in Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
where the waves bring conches and pile them on the shore.
Go and bow to him, fold your hands and prise him for me also."
3348 pōṟṟi yāṉ irantēṉ puṉṉimēl uṟi pūṅ kuyilkāḷ
cēṟṟil vāḷi tuḷḷum tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr uṟiyum
āṟṟal āḻi aṅki amarar perumāṉik kaṇṭu
māṟṟam koṇṭaruḷīr miyal tīrvatu oruvaṇṇamē (6)
3348. She says, "O beautiful cuckoo birds.
You stay in the punni trees.
I prise you and implore you.
He is the highest god of the gods in the sky
and he carries a powerful discus in his beautiful hands.
He is the lord of divine Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
where the vali fish frolic in the muddy water.
Go see him and tell him how I suffer from his love.
Bring me his reply so my pin will go away."
3349 oruvaṇṇam ceṉṟu pukku eṉakku oṉṟu uri oṇ kiḷiyē
ceru oṇ pūm poḻil cūḻ cekkar vēlit tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr
karu vaṇṇam ceyya vāy ceyya kaṇ ceyya ki ceyya kāl
ceru oṇ cakkaram caṅku aṭiyāḷam tiruntak kaṇṭē (7)
3349. She says, "O lovely parrot, go directly, without wandering,
and enter Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur where my god stays
surrounded by shores with red sand
and groves blooming with lovely flowers.
He has a dark color, a red mouth, lovely eyes,
reddish hands and beautiful feet
and he carries in his hand a shining discus for fighting and a conch.
That is how you can recognize him.
Tell him that I love him."
3350 tiruntak kaṇṭu eṉakku oṉṟu uriyāy oṇ ciṟu pūvāy
cerunti ñāḻal makiḻ puṉṉi cūḻ taṇ tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr
perum taṇ tāmarikkaṇ peru nīḷ muṭi nāl taṭantōḷ
karun tiṇ mā mukil pōl tirumēṉi aṭikaḷiyē (8)
3350. She says, "O lovely little puvi bird,
my god has large beautiful lotus eyes,
long hir, four strong arms,
and a dark divine body the color of thick clouds.
He stays in rich Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
surrounded by mahizh, punni, nyazhal and cherunti trees.
See him, tell him of my love
and then return and tell me what he has told you."
3351 aṭikaḷ kitoḻutu alarmēl aciyum aṉṉaṅkāḷ
viṭivi caṅku olikkum tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr uṟiyum
kaṭiya māyaṉ taṉṉi kaṇṇaṉi neṭumālik kaṇṭu
koṭiya valviṉiyēṉ tiṟam kūṟumiṉ vēṟukoṇṭē (9)
3351. She says, "O swans, lotus flowers are your home.
The god, the tricky Maayan, Nedumaal, Kaṇṇan
stays in Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
where conches are blown in the morning.
I have terrible karma.
Go, see the lord and worship him.
Talk to him secretly and tell him how I am suffering."
3352 vēṟukoṇṭu ummi yāṉ irantēṉ veṟi vaṇṭiṉaṅkāḷ
tēṟu nīrp pampi vaṭapālit tiruvaṇvaṇṭūr
māṟu il pōr arakkaṉ matiḷ nīṟu eḻac ceṟṟu ukanta
ēṟu cēvakaṉārkku eṉṉiyum uḷaḷ eṉmiṉkaḷē (10)
3352. She says, "O swarm of bees
flying around fragrant flowers,
I beg you to do me favor.
The god who destroyed the forts of Lanka
of the Rakshasa king Ravana whom no enemies could subdue
stays in Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur on the bank
of the northern Pampa river that flows with abundant water.
Go and tell that victorious hero that I am still alive."
3353 miṉ koḷ cēr purinūl kuṟaḷ āy akal ñālam koṇṭa
vaṉ kaḷvaṉ aṭimēl kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ coṉṉa
paṇ koḷ āyirattuḷ ivi pattum tiruvaṇvaṇṭūrkku
iṉkoḷ pāṭal vallār mataṉar miṉṉiṭi yavarkkē (11)
3353. Sadagopan of Thirukuruhur composed
a thousand poems with music on the god,
the cunning thief who went to Mahabali as a dwarf
adorned with a sacred shining thread
and took over the wide world in two footsteps.
If devotees learn and recite these ten musical pasurams
on the god of Thiruvaṇvaṇḍur
they will be like Kama for women with waists thin as lightning.
--------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (iraṇṭām tirumoḻi, 3354-3364)
talivaṉ kālam tāḻttu varakkaṇṭa talivi ūṭalkoṇṭu urittal
3354 miṉ iṭi maṭavārkaḷ niṉ aruḷ cūṭuvār muṉpu
nāṉ atu añcuvaṉ
maṉ uṭi ilaṅki araṇ kāynta māyavaṉē
uṉṉuṭiya cuṇṭāyam nāṉ aṟivaṉ iṉi
atukoṇṭu ceyvatu eṉ?
eṉṉuṭiya pantum kaḻalum tantu pōku nampī (1)
3354. She says, "You are Maayavan
and you burned the forts in Lanka of the king Ravaṇa.
I am afrid when I see the girls
whose waists are thin as lightning
that you have cheated, loving them and leaving them.
I know how you cheat.
What are you going to do now to cheat me?
O Nambi, give me back my ball and dice and then go."
3355 pōku nampī uṉ tāmaripuri kaṇ iṇiyum
cevvāy muṟuvalum
ākulaṅkaḷ ceyya aḻitaṟkē nōṟṟōmē yām
tōki mā mayilārkaḷ niṉ aruḷ cūṭuvār
cevi ōci vittu eḻa
ākaḷ pōkaviṭṭu kuḻal ūtu pōyiruntē (2)
3355. She says, "O Nambi, go away.
Your lotus eyes, red mouth and smile bother us.
We did so much tapas to love you
but you just give us trouble.
There are other girls, as lovely as peacocks,
waiting to love you.
They want to hear the sound of your flute.
Go, graze your cows and play your flute
and go to them. Please go away."
3356 pōyiruntu niṉ puḷḷuvam aṟiyātavarkku uri
nampī niṉ ceyya
vāy iruṅ kaṉiyum kaṇkaḷum viparītam in nāḷ
vēy irum taṭam tōḷiṉār it tiruvaruḷ
peṟuvār evarkol
mā irum kaṭalik kaṭinta perumāṉālē? (3)
3356. She says, "O Nambi, go and tell your lies
to those who do not know how you cheat girls.
Your big sweet fruit-like mouth and large eyes are dangerous!
Who among the girls with round bamboo-like arms
has enough tapas to receive your divine grace?
You are the highest god and you churned the large milky ocean."
3357 āliṉ nīḷ ili ēḻ ulakum uṇṭu aṉṟu nī
kiṭantāy uṉ māyaṅkaḷ
mēli vāṉavarum aṟiyār iṉi em paramē?
vēliṉ nēr taṭam kaṇṇiṉār viḷiyāṭu
cūḻalic cūḻavē niṉṟu
kāli mēykka vallāy emmi nī kaḻaṟēlē (4)
3357. She says, "You swallowed all the seven worlds
and recline on a banyan leaf.
Even the gods in the sky do not know your magic.
O my highest lord,
you are skilled at grazing cows
while cowherd girls with large lovely spear-like eyes
stay around you and play.
Don’t try to play with me."
3358 kaḻaṟēl nampī uṉ kitavam maṇṇum viṇṇum
naṉku aṟiyum tiṇ cakkara
niḻaṟu tol paṭiyāy uṉakku oṉṟu uṇarttuvaṉ nāṉ
maḻaṟu tēṉ moḻiyārkaḷ niṉ aruḷ cūṭuvār
maṉam vāṭi niṟka em
kuḻaṟu pūviyoṭum kiḷiyōṭum kuḻakēlē (5)
3358. She says, "O Nambi, don’t lie and cheat us.
All the world and the sky know your tricks.
You carry a powerful, ancient discus.
I want to tell you something.
The girls who speak like babies
with their honey-like sweet soft words
are upset and wait for your grace.
Don’t play with our prattling puvi birds and our parrots."
3359 kuḻaki eṅkaḷ kuḻamaṇaṉkoṇṭu kōyiṉmi ceytu
kaṉmam oṉṟu illi
paḻaki yām iruppōm paramē it tiru aruḷkaḷ?
aḻakiyār iv ulakam mūṉṟukkum tēvimi
ītakuvār palar uḷar
kaḻakam ēṟēl nampī uṉakkum iḷitē kaṉmamē (6)
3359. She says, "There is no use making faces
and doing tricks to make us think you love us.
We have been with you for a long time.
O highest god! Is this is way you give your grace?
There are many lovely women
as beautiful as goddesses of the three worlds
and worthy of your love.
Don’t play with us.
It is all right to play like this becuse you are young."
3360 kaṉmam aṉṟu eṅkaḷ kiyil pāvi paṟippatu
kaṭal ñālam uṇṭiṭṭa
niṉmalā neṭiyāy uṉakkēlum piḻi piḻiyē
vaṉmamē colli emmi nī viḷiyāṭuti atu
kēṭkil eṉ aimmār
taṉma pāvam eṉṉār oru nāṉṟu taṭi piṇakkē (7)
3360. She says, "It is not right to grab the dolls
that we hold in our hands to play with.
O lord, you are faultless and you swallowed the world
surrounded by oceans.
O Neḍumaal, even if you do something wrong,
mistakes are mistakes.
You play with us and cheat us with your sweet words.
If my brothers hear what you say
they won’t consider whether it is right or not to act.
They will take sticks and come to fight with you."
3361 piṇakki yāviyum yāvarum piḻiyāmal pētittum
pētiyātatu ōr
kaṇakku il kīrtti veḷḷak katir ñāṉa mūrttiyiṉāy
iṇakki emmi em tōḻimār viḷiyāṭap pōtumiṉ
eṉṉap pōntōmi
uṇakki nī vaḷittāl eṉ collār ukavātavarē? (8)
3361. She says, "Your form is like a flood of wisdom
and you shine with endless fame.
Without fail you create both creatures that are intelligent
and creatures that are ignorant.
My friends are calling me to go play with them
but you come and attract me with your love
so I can’t join them.
If people see me with you, what will they think?"
3362 ukaviyāl neñcam uḷ uruki uṉ tāmarit
taṭam kaṇ viḻikaḷiṉ
aka valip paṭuppāṉ aḻittāy uṉ tiruvaṭiyāl
takavu ceytili eṅkaḷ ciṟṟilum yām aṭu
ciṟu cōṟum kaṇṭu niṉ
muka oḷi tikaḻa muṟuval ceytu niṉṟiliyē (9)
3362. She says, "You attract us with your large lotus eyes
and you want us to fall in love with you, melting in our hearts.
You kicked and broke our small play houses with your divine feet.
That wasn’t fair.
You didn’t look at our play houses and the food we made for them.
You didn’t just stand there with your shining face and smile at us,
but you kicked over our small play houses.
That wasn’t right."
3363 niṉṟu ilaṅku muṭiyiṉāy irupattōr kāl
aracu kaḷikaṭṭa
veṉṟi nīḷ maḻuvā viyaṉ ñālam muṉ paṭittāy
iṉṟu iv āyar kulatti vīṭu uyyat tōṉṟiya
karumāṇikkac cuṭar
niṉtaṉṉāl nalivē paṭuvōm eṉṟum āycciyōmē (10)
3363. She says, "You have a shining crown.
You carry a mazhu weapon
that conquered and destroyed the kings of thirty-one generations.
You created this wide world in ancient times.
You are a bright dark diamond.
You were born among the cowherd families to give them moksha,
but see, you are nughty
and you always give us cowherd girls trouble."
3364 āycci ākiya aṉṉiyāl aṉṟu veṇṇey
vārttiyuḷ cīṟṟa muṇṭu aḻu
kūtta appaṉ taṉṉi kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ
ēttiya tamiḻ māli āyirattuḷ iviyum ōr pattu
ici yoṭum
nāttaṉṉāl navila urippārkku illi nalkuravē (11)
3364. Sadagopan of Thirukuruhur
composed a thousand pasurams with music
on the god, our father, the dancer
who cried when his mother Yashoda, the cowherdess,
was angry with him becuse he stole butter and tied him up.
If devotees learn and recite with their tongues these ten pasurams
they will have no troubles in their lives.
---------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (mūṉṟām tirumoḻi, 3365-3375)
tammi vacīkarittavaṉ sarvē#varaṉ eṉṟu aruḷic ceytal (tiruviṇṇakar)
3365 nalkuravum celvum narakum cuvarkkamum āy
velpakiyum naṭpum viṭamum amutamum āy
palvakiyum paranta perumāṉ eṉṉi āḷvāṉi
celvam malku kuṭit tiruviṇṇakark kaṇṭēṉē (1)
3365. The god is poverty and wealth.
He is heaven and hell.
He is friend and enemy.
He is nectar and poison.
The lord is omnipresent.
He rules me—I saw my lord in Thiruviṇṇagar
where families live with abundant wealth.
3366 kaṇṭa iṉpam tuṉpam kalakkaṅkaḷum tēṟṟamum āy
taṇṭamum taṇmiyum taḻalum niḻalum āy
kaṇṭukōṭaṟku ariya perumāṉ eṉṉi āḷvāṉ ūr
teṇ tirip puṉal cūḻ tiruviṇṇakar nal nakarē (2)
3366. He is joy and sorrow.
He is confusion and clarity.
He is anger and affection.
He is heat and coolness.
He is the lord no one can see.
The god of beautiful Thiruviṇṇagar,
surrounded by water with clear waves,
is my ruler.
3367 nakaramum nāṭukaḷum ñāṉamum mūṭamum āy
nikar il cūḻ cuṭar āy iruḷ āy nilaṉ āy vicumpu āy
cikara māṭaṅkaḷ cūḻ tiruviṇṇakar cērnta pirāṉ
pukar koḷ kīrtti allāl illi yāvarkkum puṇṇiyamē (3)
3367. He is countries and cities.
He is wisdom and ignorance.
He is unmatched enveloping light and darkness.
He is the earth and sky.
There is no puṇṇiyam for anyone
without prising the fame of the lord
of Thiruviṇṇagar surrounded by palaces.
3368 puṇṇiyam pāvam puṇarcci pirivu eṉṟu ivi āy
eṇṇam āy maṟappu āy uṇmi āy iṉmi āy allaṉ āy
tiṇṇa māṭaṅkaḷ cūḻ tiruviṇṇakar cērnta pirāṉ
kaṇṇaṉ iṉ aruḷē kaṇṭukoḷmiṉkaḷ kitavamē? (4)
3368. The god is virtue and sin.
He is being together and separation.
He is remembrance and forgetfulness.
He is all that is in the world
and he is all that is not in the world.
He is existence, non-existence and nothing.
Find the sweet grace of Kaṇṇan,
our dear god of Thiruviṇṇagar
surrounded by strong palaces,
and take on yourself his cleverness.
3369 kitavam cemmi karumi veḷumiyum āy
mey poy iḷami mutumi putumi paḻamiyum āy
ceyta tiṇ matiḷ cūḻ tiruviṇṇakar cērnta pirāṉ
peyta kāvu kaṇṭīr perum tēvu uṭi mūvulakē (5)
3369. He is bad and good.
He is black and white.
He is truth and falsehood.
He is youth and age.
He is new and old.
Our god stays in Thiruviṇṇagar surrounded by strong walls.
He is the great god of the three worlds,
and see, he will protect all the worlds.
3370 mūvulakaṅkaḷum āy allaṉ āy ukappu āy muṉivu āy
pūvil vāḻ makaḷ āy tavvi āy pukaḻ āy paḻi āy
tēvar mēvit toḻum tiruviṇṇakar cērnta pirāṉ
pāviyēṉ maṉattē uṟikiṉṟa parañcuṭarē (6)
3370. He is all the three worlds and he is not them.
He is happiness and hatred.
He is Lakshmi, the goddess on a lotus.
He is the inuspicious goddess.
He is fame and he is infamy.
The gods in the sky come to worship
the lord of Thiruvinnagar, the highest light,
and he stays in this sinner’s mind.
3371 param cuṭar uṭampu āy aḻukkup patitta uṭampu āy
karantum tōṉṟiyum niṉṟum kitavaṅkaḷ ceytum viṇṇōr
ciraṅkaḷāl vaṇaṅkum tiruviṇṇakar cērnta pirāṉ
varam koḷ pātam allāl illi yāvarkkum vaṉ caraṇē (7)
3371. He takes shining divine forms.
He takes forms that are unclean.
He hides himself.
He presents himself to his devotees .
He stays in the world.
He does nughty deeds.
The gods from the sky come and worship,
bowing their heads to our god of Thiruviṇṇagar.
There is no refuge for anyone but the feet of the lord
who give boons to all.
3372 vaṉ caraṇ curarkku āy acurarkku vem kūṟṟamum āy
taṉ caraṇ niḻaṟkīḻ ulakam vittum viyātum
teṉ caraṇ ticikkut tiruviṇṇakar cērnta pirāṉ
eṉ caraṇ eṉ kaṇṇaṉ eṉṉi āḷuṭi eṉ appaṉē (8)
3372. He gives refuge to the gods.
He is cruel Yama for the Asurans.
He keeps good people beneath his feet and protects them
but he does not protect those who are bad.
Our lord stays in Thiruviṇṇagar
and protect the southern direction.
He is my father and he rules me.
My god Kanṇan is my refuge.
3373 eṉ appaṉ eṉakku āy ikuḷ āy eṉṉip peṟṟavaḷ āy
poṉ appaṉ maṇi appaṉ muttu appaṉ aṉ appaṉum āy
miṉṉap poṉ matiḷ cūḻ tiruviṇṇakar cērnta appaṉ
taṉ oppār il appaṉ tantaṉaṉ taṉa tāḷ niḻalē (9)
3373. He is my father.
He is my friend.
He is the mother who gave birth to me.
He my father is precious as gold, diamonds and pearl.
He stays in Thiruviṇṇagar
surrounded with shining golden walls.
He, the matchless god, granted me refuge beneath his feet.
3374 niḻal veyyil ciṟumi perumi kuṟumi neṭumiyum āy
cuḻalvaṉa niṟpaṉa maṟṟum āy avi allaṉum āy
maḻali vāy vaṇṭu vāḻ tiruviṇṇakar maṉṉu pirāṉ
kaḻalkaḷ aṉṟi maṟṟōr kaḷikaṇ ilam kāṇmiṉkaḷē (10)
3374. He is shadow and sunlight.
He is small and great.
He is short and tall.
He is those things that move and those that are still,
and he is everything other than those things.
He stays always in Thiruviṇṇagar
where the bees sing as sweetly as babies.
I have no other refuge
than his feet ornamented with anklets.
3375 kāṇmiṉkaḷ ulakīr eṉṟu kaṇmukappē nimirnta
tāḷ iṇiyaṉ taṉṉik kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ coṉṉa
āṇi āyirattut tiruviṇṇakarp pattum vallār
kōṇi iṉṟi viṇṇōrkku eṉṟum āvar kuravarkaḷē (11)
3375. Sadagopan of Thirukuruhur
composed a thousand poems on the lord
who measured the world and the sky with his two feet
and sid, "O people of the world, see my feet!"
If devotees learn and recite these ten pasurams on Thiruviṇṇagar
they will become the gurus of the gods in the sky.
---------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (nāṉkām tirumoḻi, 3376-3386)
kaṇṇaṉatu avatārac ceyalkaḷip pēcap peṟṟamikkuk kaḷittal
3376 kuravi āycciyarōṭu kōttatum
kuṉṟam oṉṟu ēntiyatum
uravu nīrp poyki nākam kāyntatum
uṭpaṭa maṟṟum pala
aravil paḷḷip pirāṉ taṉ māya
viṉikaḷiyē alaṟṟi
iravum naṉ pakalum tavirkilaṉ
eṉṉa kuṟi eṉakkē? (1)
3376. Our lord danced the kuravi dance with the cowherd girls.
He carried Govardhana mountin to protect the cows.
He danced on the head of the snake Kalingan
in the pond with roaring water.
The lord sleeps on a snake bed and I prattle night and day,
telling all his magic deeds without stopping.
What bad things can happen to me from now on?
3377 kēyat tīm kuḻal ūtiṟṟum niri mēyttatum
keṇṭi oṇ kaṇ
vācap pūṅ kuḻal piṉṉi tōḷkaḷ
maṇantatum maṟṟum pala
māyak kōlap pirāṉ taṉ ceyki
niṉintu maṉam kuḻintu
nēyattōṭu kaḻinta pōtu eṉakku
ev ulakam nikarē? (2)
3376. Our lord danced the kuravi dance with the cowherd girls.
He carried Govardhana mountin to protect the cows.
He danced on the head of the snake Kalingan
in the pond with roaring water.
The lord sleeps on a snake bed and I prattle night and day,
telling all his magic deeds without stopping.
What bad things can happen to me from now on?
3378 nikar il mallaric ceṟṟatum niri mēyttatum
nīḷ neṭum kic
cikara mā kaḷiṟu aṭṭatum ivi
pōlvaṉavum piṟavum
pukarkoḷ cōtip pirāṉ taṉ ceyki
niṉintu pulampi eṉṟum
nukara vikal vikappeṟṟēṉ eṉakku
eṉ iṉi nōvatuvē? (3)
3378. He killed the matchless heroic wrestlers.
He grazed the herd of cows.
He killed the angry long-trunked elephant Kuvalayabeeḍam.
I think constantly and prattle about these deeds
and others like them of the radiant lord prised by all.
He gives his grace to me every day.
What suffering can there be for me now?
3379 nōva āycci uralōṭu ārkka iraṅkiṟṟum
vañcap peṇṇic
cāvap pāl uṇṭatum ūr cakaṭam
iṟac cāṭiyatum
tēvak kōlap pirāṉ taṉ ceyki
niṉintu maṉam kuḻintu
mēvak kālaṅkaḷ kūṭiṉēṉ eṉakku
eṉ iṉi vēṇṭuvatē? (4)
3379. He cried when Yashoda tied him to a mortar.
He drank the milk of the cheating devil Puthana.
Sakaṭasuran came in the form of a cart and he kicked him.
When I think of all the heroic deeds of the beautiful god of gods
my heart melts and is constantly with the god.
What do I want any more?
3380 vēṇṭit tēvar irakka vantu piṟantatum
vīṅku iruḷvāy
pūṇṭu aṉṟu aṉṉip pulampa pōy aṅku ōr
āykkulam pukkatum
kāṇṭal iṉṟi vaḷarntu kañcaṉit tuñca
vañcam ceytatum
īṇṭu nāṉ alaṟṟappeṟṟēṉ eṉakku
eṉṉa ikal uḷatē? (5)
3380. He was born as Kaṇṇan
when the gods asked him to come and destroy the Rakshasas.
While his mother suffered and cried
his father Vasudeva took him in the darkness
to a cowherd village where he was rised.
Kamsan did not know Kaṇṇan was growing up in the cowherd village
and that his tricks would destroy him.
I prattle on thinking of all his heroic deeds.
How could there be any enemy for me?
3381 ikal koḷ puḷḷip piḷantatum imil
ēṟukaḷ ceṟṟatuvum
uyar koḷ cōlik kuruntu ocittatum
uṭpaṭa maṟṟum pala
akal koḷ viyam aḷanta māyaṉ eṉ
appaṉ taṉ māyaṅkaḷē
pakal irāp paravap peṟṟēṉ eṉakku
eṉṉa maṉap parippē? (6)
3381. When an Asuran came as his enemy in the form of a bird
he split open its beak and killed it.
He killed seven bulls to marry Nappinni.
He broke the kurundam trees that grew tall in the groves.
He is the Maayan who measured the wide world at the sacrifice of
Mahabali.
Night and day I prise the magical deeds of my father the god.
I will have no trouble in my heart.
3382 maṉap parippōṭu aḻukku māṉiṭa
cātiyil tāṉpiṟantu
taṉakku vēṇṭu urukkoṇṭu tāṉ taṉa
cīṟṟattiṉi muṭikkum
puṉat tuḻāy muṭi māli mārpaṉ eṉ
appaṉ taṉ māyaṅkaḷē
niṉikkum neñcu uṭiyēṉ eṉakku
iṉi yār nikar nīḷ nilattē? (7)
3382. He showed his compassion by being born
to help the evil people of the earth.
He took any form he wished.
My father, adorned with a forest thulasi garland on his chest,
came to the earth becuse he was angry at the evil Rakshasas.
I think constantly of the magical deeds of my father, the lord.
Who is there as fortunate as I?
3383 nīḷ nilattoṭu vāṉ viyappa niṟi perum
pōrkaḷ ceytu
vāṇaṉ āyiram tōḷ tuṇittatum
uṭpaṭa maṟṟum pala
māṇi āy nilam koṇṭa māyaṉ eṉ
appaṉ taṉ māyaṅkaḷē
kāṇum neñcu uṭiyēṉ eṉakku
iṉi eṉṉa kalakkam uṇṭē? (8)
3383. He fought many terrific battles, amazing the world.
He cut off the thousand arms of Vaṇasuran.
He measured the earth and sky at the sacrifice of Mahabali.
I know all the magical deeds of my father, the Maayan
and there is no distress in my mind anymore.
3384 kalakka ēḻ kaṭal ēḻ mali ulaku ēḻum
kaḻiyak kaṭāy
ulakkat tērkoṭu ceṉṟa māyamum
uṭpaṭa maṟṟum pala
valakki āḻi iṭakkic caṅkam
ivi uṭi mālvaṇṇaṉi
malakkum nā uṭiyēṟku māṟu uḷatō
im maṇṇiṉ miciyē? (9)
3384. He swallowed all the seven seas, the seven mountins
and the seven worlds.
He drove the chariot for Arjuna with his maayam.
He carries the discus in his right hand and the conch in his left,
I prattle, prising the dark-colored god with my tongue
and that is all I want to do.
3385 maṇmicip perum pāram nīṅka ōr
pārata mā perum pōr
paṇṇi māyaṅkaḷ ceytu cēṉiyip pāḻpaṭa
nūṟṟiṭṭup pōy
viṇmicit taṉa tāmamē puka mēviya
cōti taṉ tāḷ
naṇṇi nāṉ vaṇaṅkappeṟṟēṉ eṉakku
ār piṟar nāyakarē? (10)
3385. He made Arjuna fight in the terrible Bharatha war.
He performed many magic deeds and destroyed the Kaurava army.
He is a bright light in the sky and he wears the garland of victory.
I approached and worshiped him.
Who could be my lord in this world but he?
3386 nāyakaṉ muḻu ēḻ ulakukkum
āy muḻu ēḻ ulakum taṉ
vāyakam puka vittu umiḻntu
avi āy avi allaṉum ām
kēcavaṉ aṭi iṇimicik
kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ coṉṉa
tūya āyirattu ip pattāl
pattar āvar tuvaḷ iṉṟiyē (11)
3386. Sagagopan of Thirukuruhur worshiped the feet
of the lord Kesavan and composed a thousand poems.
The lord of all the seven worlds swallowed them all,
kept them in his stomach and at end of the world
he spit them out from his mouth.
If devotees learn and recite these ten pasurams
they will become faultless devotees of the god.
-----------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (aintām tirumoḻi, 3387-3397)
tōḻi tāymāri nōkkik kūṟutal (tolivillimaṅkalam)
3387 tuvaḷ il mā maṇi māṭam ōṅku
tolivillimaṅkalam toḻum
ivaḷi nīr iṉi aṉṉimīr umakku
āci illi viṭumiṉō
tavaḷa oṇ caṅku cakkaram eṉṟum
tāmarit taṭam kaṇ eṉṟum
kuvaḷi oṇ malark kaṇkaḷ nīr malka
niṉṟu niṉṟu kumuṟumē (1)
3387. Her friends say,
"O mothers! She worships Tholivillimangalam
filled with palaces studded with bright faultless diamonds.
O mothers, you took her to Tholivillimangalam
and brought her back
and now she has fallen in love with the god and says,
‘He carries a shining conch that gives birth to pearls,
and a discus in his hands.
His big eyes are beautiful as lotuses.’
Her eyes, as beautiful as kuvali flowers, are filled with tears
and she stands and cries."
3388 kumuṟum ōci viḻavu olit
tolivillimaṅkalam koṇṭupukku
amuta meṉ moḻiyāḷi nīr umakku
āci iṉṟi akaṟṟiṉīr
timir koṇṭāl ottu niṟkum maṟṟu ivaḷ
tēva tēvapirāṉ eṉṟē
nimiyum vāyoṭu kaṇkaḷ nīr malka
nekku ocintu kariyumē (2)
3388. Her friends say,
"O mothers, you took your dughter with soft words sweet as nectar
to Tholivillimangalam where the noise of festivals is loud
and you brought her back.
She has fallen in love with the god.
She stands, unable to do anything,
and she says, ‘You are the god of gods!’
Her eyes are filled with tears and she melts with love for him."
3389 kari koḷ pim poḻil taṇ paṇit
tolivillimaṅkalam koṇṭupukku
uri koḷ iṉ moḻiyāḷi nīr umakku
āci iṉṟi akaṟṟiṉīr
tiri koḷ puvattuc cērntatum
tici ñālam tāvi aḷantatum
nirikaḷ mēyttatumē pitaṟṟi
neṭum kaṇ nīr malka niṟkumē (3)
3389. Her friends say,
"O mothers, your dughter’s words are sweet as nectar.
You took her to Thirutholivillimangalam
on the banks of the Thamiraparaṇi river,
with flourishing groves and cool paddy fields,
and you brought her back
even though she didn’t want to return.
She says, ‘He sleeps on the milky ocean roaring with waves.
He measured the earth and the sky.
He grazes the cows.’
She prattles on and her long eyes are filled with tears."
3390 niṟkum nālmaṟivāṇar vāḻ
tolivillimaṅkalam kaṇṭapiṉ
aṟkam oṉṟum aṟa uṟāḷ malintāḷ
kaṇṭīr ivaḷ aṉṉimīr
kaṟkum kalvi ellām karuṅ kaṭal
vaṇṇaṉ kaṇṇa pirāṉ eṉṟē
oṟkam oṉṟum ilaḷ ukantu ukantu
uḷ makiḻntu kuḻiyumē (4)
3390. Her friends say,
"O mothers, look.
You took her to Thirutholivillimangalam
where Brahmins who know all four Vedas live.
After she returned she has forgotten how to be shy.
She doesn’t want to listen to you but says,
‘All that I have learned and know is about the dark-colored Kaṇṇan.’
She repeats this agin and agin, never growing tired.
In her heart she is happy and melts for him."
3391 kuḻiyum vāḷ mukattu ēḻiyit
tolivillimaṅkalam koṇṭupukku
iḻi koḷ cōtic centāmarik kaṇ
pirāṉ iruntami kāṭṭiṉīr
maḻi peytāl okkum kaṇṇa nīriṉoṭu
aṉṟu toṭṭum miyāntu ivaḷ
nuḻiyum cintiyaḷ aṉṉimīr toḻum
at tici uṟṟu nōkkiyē (5)
3391. Her friends say,
"O mothers, your innocent dughter has a gentle shining face.
You took her to Tholivillimangalam
and showed her the beautiful lotus-eyed lord
adorned with shining ornaments.
Her eyes shed tears like rin.
Her mind is fascinated with the qualities of the god
and thinks only of him.
She constantly looks in the direction where god stays
and worships him."
3392 nōkkum pakkam ellām karumpoṭu
cennel ōṅku centāmari
vāykkum taṇ porunal vaṭakari
vaṇ tolivillimaṅkalam
nōkkumēl at tici allāl maṟu
nōkku ilaḷ vikal nāḷtoṟum
vāykkoḷ vācakamum maṇivaṇṇaṉ
nāmamē ivaḷ aṉṉimīr! (6)
3391. Her friends say,
"O mothers, your innocent dughter has a gentle shining face.
You took her to Tholivillimangalam
and showed her the beautiful lotus-eyed lord
adorned with shining ornaments.
Her eyes shed tears like rin.
Her mind is fascinated with the qualities of the god
and thinks only of him.
She constantly looks in the direction where god stays
and worships him."
3393 aṉṉimīr aṇi mā mayil ciṟumāṉ
ivaḷ nammik kivalintu
eṉṉa vārttiyum kēṭkuṟāḷ
tolivillimaṅkalam eṉṟu allāl
muṉṉam nōṟṟa vitikolō? mukil
vaṇṇaṉ māyam kolō? avaṉ
ciṉṉamum tirunāmamum ivaḷ
vāyaṉakaḷ tiruntavē (7)
3393. Her friends say,
"O mothers, your dughter is as beautiful as a peacock
and innocent as a small deer.
She refuses to listen to anything we say
but only repeats the word ‘Tholivillimangalam.’
Is this the fruit of the tapas that she did in her last birth
or is it the magic of the cloud-colored god?
She constantly speaks of his divine name
and his discus, sword, club and conch.
Her mouth can only say the god’s names clearly."
3394 tiruntu vētamum vēḷviyum
tiru mā makaḷirum tām malintu
iruntu vāḻ porunal vaṭakari
vaṇ tolivillimaṅkalam
karun taṭam kaṇṇi kitoḻuta an nāḷ
toṭaṅki in nāḷtoṟum
iruntu iruntu aravintalōcaṉa
eṉṟu eṉṟē nintu iraṅkumē (8)
3393. Her friends say,
"O mothers, your dughter is as beautiful as a peacock
and innocent as a small deer.
She refuses to listen to anything we say
but only repeats the word ‘Tholivillimangalam.’
Is this the fruit of the tapas that she did in her last birth
or is it the magic of the cloud-colored god?
She constantly speaks of his divine name
and his discus, sword, club and conch.
Her mouth can only say the god’s names clearly."
3395 iraṅki nāḷtoṟum vāyverīi ivaḷ
kaṇṇa nīrkaḷ alamara
maraṅkaḷum iraṅkum vaki
maṇivaṇṇavō eṉṟu kūvumāl
turaṅkam vāy piḷantāṉ uṟi
tolivillimaṅkalam eṉṟu taṉ
karaṅkaḷ kūppit toḻum av ūrt
tirunāmam kaṟṟataṉ piṉṉiyē (9)
3395. Her friends say,
"O mothers, she worries every day
and is afrid to say anything.
Her eyes are filled with tears.
When she calls out saying, ‘O dark diamond-colored god!’
even the trees feel sorry for her.
Ever since she learned the divine name of the city
she has worshiped the god folding her hands
and sid, ‘That is Tholivillimangalam of the god
who split open the Asuran’s mouth
when he came in the form of a horse.’"
3396 piṉṉikol nila mā makaḷkol
tirumakaḷkol piṟantiṭṭāḷ?
eṉṉa māyamkolō ivaḷ neṭumāl
eṉṟē niṉṟu kūvumāl
muṉṉi vantu avaṉ niṉṟu iruntu
uṟiyum tolivillimaṅkalam
ceṉṉiyāl vaṇaṅkum av ūrt
tirunāmam kēṭpatu cintiyē. (10)
3396. Her friends say,
"O mothers, is she Nappinni?
Is she the great earth goddess?
Is she beautiful Lakshmi?
She was born on this earth—what magic is this?
She calls to him loudly, ‘O Neḍumaal!’
Her heart wants to hear the divine names of the lord
of Tholivillimangalam
and she worships him bowing her head."
3397 cintiyālum collālum
ceykiyiṉālum tēva pirāṉiyē
tanti tāy eṉṟu aṭinta
vaṇ kurukūravar caṭakōpaṉ
munti āyirattuḷ ivi toli
villimaṅkalattic coṉṉa
centamiḻp pattum vallār
aṭimiceyvār tirumālukkē (11)
3397. Sadagopan of rich Thirukuruhur
worships the god with his thoughts, words and deeds.
He prised the god, his father and mother.
He composed a thousand poems
and among them ten prise the god in Tholivillimangalam.
If devotees learn and recite these ten pure Tamil pasurams
they will become the devotees of Thirumaal.
-----------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (āṟām tirumoḻi, 3398-3408)
talivaṉatu pirivāl varuntum talimakaḷik kuṟittut tāy iraṅkutal
3398 mālukku viyam aḷanta maṇāḷaṟku
nīlak karu niṟa mēka niyāyaṟku
kōlac centāmarik kaṇṇaṟku eṉ koṅku alar
ēlak kuḻali iḻantatu caṅkē (1)
3398. Her mother says,
"The god measured the world at Mahabali’s sacrifice.
My dughter is decorated with lovely flowers
dripping with honey in her hir.
She has grown thin and her conch bangles have become loose
and fallen from her arms
becuse she loves the beautiful god, the lotus-eyed Kaṇṇan,
the dark cloud-colored Maal."
3399 caṅku vil vāḷ taṇṭu cakkarak kiyaṟku
ceṅkaṉivāyc ceyya tāmarik kaṇṇaṟku
koṅku alar taṇ am tuḻāy muṭiyāṉukku eṉ
maṅki iḻantatu māmi niṟamē (2)
3399. Her mother says,
"The god carries in his hands
a conch, bow, sword, club and a discus.
His mouth is sweet as a fruit.
His eyes are beautiful as lotuses,
and he is adorned with a cool thulasi garland
blooming with flowers that drip honey.
My dughter has lost her dark color and become pale
becuse she is in love with him."
3400 niṟam kariyāṉukku nīṭu ulaku uṇṭa
tiṟam kiḷar vāyc ciṟuk kaḷvaṉ avaṟku
kaṟaṅkiya cakkarak kiyavaṉukku eṉ
piṟaṅku irum kūntal iḻantatu pīṭē (3)
3400. Her mother says,
"He, the dark god, the little thief who stole butter,
swallowed the wide world with his beautiful red mouth.
He has a whirling discus in his hand.
My girl with long beautiful hir
has lost her pride becuse she loves the dark-colored god."
3401 pīṭu uṭi nāṉmukaṉip paṭittāṉukku
māṭu uṭi viyam aḷanta maṇāḷaṟku
nāṭu uṭi maṉṉarkkut tūtu cel nampikku eṉ
pāṭu uṭi alkul iḻantatu paṇpē (4)
3401. He created the famous Nanmuhan.
He measured the flourishing world
and went as a messenger to the Kauravas for the Pandavas
to ask for their lands.
My dughter has lost the beauty of her wide hips
becuse she loves Nambi.
3402 paṇpu uṭi vētam payanta paraṉukku
maṇ puri viyam iṭanta varākaṟku
teṇ puṉal paḷḷi em tēva pirāṉukku eṉ
kaṇpuṉi kōti iḻantatu kaṟpē (5)
3402. He gave the marvelous Vedas to the world.
He came as a boar and split open the world
that is filled with sand.
He, the lord of the gods, sleeps on a bed on the clear ocean.
My dughter is precious as my eyes.
She has lost her chastity
becuse she loves the highest god of gods
3403 kaṟpakak kā aṉa nal pala tōḷaṟku
poṉ cuṭark kuṉṟu aṉṉa pūn taṇ muṭiyaṟku
nal pala tāmari nāḷ malark kiyaṟku eṉ
vil puruvakkoṭi tōṟṟatu meyyē (6)
3403. His many arms are like the branches of the Karpaga garden.
He wears a beautiful crown like a shining hill.
He has lovely lotus-like hands.
It is true that my lovely dughter
who has bow-shaped eyebrows has lost her beauty.
3404 mey amar pal kalaṉ naṉku aṇintāṉukku
pi araviṉ aṇip paḷḷiyiṉāṉukku
kiyoṭu kāl ceyya kaṇṇa pirāṉukku eṉ
tiyal iḻantatu taṉṉuṭic cāyē (7)
3403. His many arms are like the branches of the Karpaga garden.
He wears a beautiful crown like a shining hill.
He has lovely lotus-like hands.
It is true that my lovely dughter
who has bow-shaped eyebrows has lost her beauty.
3405 cāyak kuruntam ocitta tamiyaṟku
māyac cakaṭam utitta maṇāḷaṟku
pēyip piṇampaṭap pāl uṇ pirāṉukku eṉ
vācak kuḻali iḻantatu māṇpē (8)
3405. The matchless god broke the kurundam tree.
He kicked Sakaṭasuran and killed him.
He drank the milk from the breast of the devil Putana and killed her.
My dughter with fragrant hir has lost her pride
becuse she loves him.
3406 māṇpu ami kōlattu em māyak kuṟaḷaṟku
cēṇ cuṭark kuṉṟu aṉṉa ceñcuṭar mūrttikku
kāṇ perum tōṟṟattu em kākutta nampikku eṉ
pūṇ puṉi meṉmuli tōṟṟatu poṟpē (9)
3406. He took the form of a lovely magic dwarf
and cheated king Mahabali.
His shines like a lustrous hill.
He is the handsome Nambi of the Kahusta dynasty.
My dughter’s soft breasts
adorned with lovely ornaments have lost their beauty
becuse she loves him.
3407 poṟpu ami nīḷ muṭip pūn taṇ tuḻāyaṟku
mal poru tōḷ uṭi māyap pirāṉukku
niṟpaṉa pal uruvāy niṟkum māyaṟku eṉ
kaṟpu uṭiyāṭṭi iḻantatu kaṭṭē (10)
3406. He took the form of a lovely magic dwarf
and cheated king Mahabali.
His shines like a lustrous hill.
He is the handsome Nambi of the Kahusta dynasty.
My dughter’s soft breasts
adorned with lovely ornaments have lost their beauty
becuse she loves him.
3408 kaṭṭu eḻil cōli nal vēṅkaṭavāṇaṉik
kaṭṭu eḻil teṉ kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ col
kaṭṭu eḻil āyirattu ip pattum vallavar
kaṭṭu eḻil vāṉavar pōkam uṇpārē (11)
3408. Saḍagopan of southern Thirukuruhur
composed a thousand beautiful pasurams
on the god of Venkaṭam hill
surrounded by thick beautiful groves.
If devotees learn and recite these ten pasurams well
they will go to heaven and enjoy being with gods.
--------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (ēḻām tirumoḻi, 3409-3419)
talivaṉatu nakarnōkkic ceṉṟa makaḷik kuṟittut tāy iraṅkutal (tirukkōḷūr)
3409 uṇṇum cōṟu parukum nīr tiṉṉum veṟṟiliyum ellām
kaṇṇaṉ em perumāṉ eṉṟu eṉṟē kaṇkaḷ nīr malki
maṇṇiṉuḷ avaṉ cīr vaḷam mikkavaṉ ūr viṉavi
tiṇṇam eṉ iḷamāṉ pukum ūr tirukkōḷūrē (1)
3409. Her mother says,
"My dughter, gentle as a young deer,
says, ‘The food we eat, the water we drink
and the betelnut we chew all belong to Kaṇṇan.’
Her eyes are filled with tears.
She asks where the famous god stays
and will surely go to Thirukkoḷur."
3410 ūrum nāṭum ulakamum taṉṉippōl avaṉuṭiya
pērum tārkaḷumē pitaṟṟa kaṟpu vāṉ iṭaṟi
cērum nal vaḷam cēr paḻaṉat tirukkōḷūrkkē
pōrum kol uriyīr koṭiyēṉ koṭi pūvikaḷē? (2)
3409. Her mother says,
"My dughter, gentle as a young deer,
says, ‘The food we eat, the water we drink
and the betelnut we chew all belong to Kaṇṇan.’
Her eyes are filled with tears.
She asks where the famous god stays
and will surely go to Thirukkoḷur."
3411 pūvi piṅkiḷikaḷ pantu tūti pūm puṭṭilkaḷ
yāviyum tirumāl tirunāmaṅkaḷē kūvi eḻum eṉ
pāvi pōy iṉit taṇ paḻaṉat tirukkōḷūrkkē
kōvi vāy tuṭippa maḻikkaṇṇoṭu eṉ ceyyumkolō? (3)
3411.Her mother says,
"My dughter’s puvi birds,
green parrots, balls, wood dolls and flower baskets
all call loudly the divine names of Thirumaal with her.
Will my doll-like dughter reach Thirukoḷur surrounded by cool fields?
What can she do if her lips that are like kovi fruits throb
and her eyes shed tears like rin?"
3412 kolli eṉparkolō kuṇam mikkaṉaḷ eṉparkolō
cilli vāyp peṇṭukaḷ ayal cēri uḷḷārum? ellē
celvam malki avaṉkiṭanta tirukkōḷūrkkē
mēl iṭi nuṭaṅka iḷamāṉ cella mēviṉaḷē (4)
3411.Her mother says,
"My dughter’s puvi birds,
green parrots, balls, wood dolls and flower baskets
all call loudly the divine names of Thirumaal with her.
Will my doll-like dughter reach Thirukoḷur surrounded by cool fields?
What can she do if her lips that are like kovi fruits throb
and her eyes shed tears like rin?"
3413 mēvi nintu nintu viḷiyāṭaluṟāḷ eṉ ciṟut
tēvi pōy iṉit taṉ tirumāl tirukkōḷūril
pū iyal poḻilum taṭamum avaṉ kōyilum kaṇṭu
āvi uḷ kuḷira eṅṅaṉē ukakkumkol iṉṟē? (5)
3413. Her mother says,
"My little dughter, as beautiful as a goddess,
does not want to play with her toys
and only worries and worries.
Today when she reaches Thirukoḷur of Thirumaal
will her heart be happy to see
the blooming groves, ponds and the temple of the lord?"
3414 iṉṟu eṉakku utavātu akaṉṟa iḷamāṉ iṉip pōy
teṉ ticit tilatam aṉiya tirukkōḷūrkkē
ceṉṟu taṉ tirumāl tirukkaṇṇum cevvāyum kaṇṭu
niṉṟu niṉṟu niyum neṭum kaṇkaḷ paṉi malkavē (6)
3414. Her mother says,
"My dughter, soft as a young deer
does not want to help me in the house
and has gone to southern Thirukoḷur,
a place as important as a thilakam is for a face.
Will she stand and stand looking at the divine eyes
and the red mouth of the god
as her long eyes fill with tears and she worries?"
3415 malku nīrk kaṇṇoṭu miyal uṟṟa maṉattiṉaḷāy
allum naṉ pakalum neṭumāl eṉṟu aḻittu iṉip pōy
celvam malki avaṉ kiṭanta tirukkōḷurkkē
olki olki naṭantu eṅṅaṉē pukumkol ocintē? (7)
3415. Her mother says,
"My dughter says night and day ‘Neḍumaal!’
as her eyes fill with tears
and her heart fills with love for him.
She has walked to rich Thirukoḷur
where the lord sleeps on the ocean.
How could she go there walking slowly
and be able to enter his temple?"
3416 ocinta nuṇ iṭimēl kiyi vittu nontu nontu
kacinta neñciṉaḷāy kaṇṇa nīr tuḷumpac cellumkol
ocinta oṇ malarāḷ koḻunaṉ tirukkōḷūrkkē
kacinta neñciṉaḷāy emmi nītta em kārikiyē? (8)
3416. Her mother says,
"Will my dughter holding her thin waist with her hands
worry and fret as she walks,
her eyes filling with tears and her heart melting,
to Thirukoḷur where Lakshmi on a lovely lotus
stays happily with her husband?
Her heart sad, my young dughter has left me."
3417 kāriyam nallaṉakaḷ avi kāṇil eṉ kaṇṇaṉukku eṉṟu
īriyāy iruppāḷ itu ellām kiṭakka iṉip pōy
cēri pal paḻi tūuy irippa tirukkōḷūrkkē
nēriḻi naṭantāḷ emmi oṉṟum niṉintilaḷē (9)
3417. Her mother says,
"When my dughter sees good things,
she says, ‘This is for my Kaṇṇan!’
and she thinks of her love for him.
Lovely with her beautiful ornaments,
she walks to Thirukoḷur and doesn’t think of me.
The village is filled with gossip about her."
3418 niṉikkilēṉ teyvaṅkāḷ neṭum kaṇ iḷamāṉ iṉip pōy
aṉittu ulakum uṭiya aravintalōcaṉaṉit
tiṉittaṉiyum viṭāḷ avaṉ cēr tirukkōḷūrkkē
maṉikku vāṉ paḻiyum niṉiyāḷ cella vittaṉaḷē (10)
3418. Her mother says,
"O gods, I cannot think of all the things she is doing.
My young deer-like dughter has long eyes
and she doesn’t stop even for a moment
thinking of the lotus-eyed god, the ruler of the whole world.
She has gone to Thirukoḷur where he stays
and she doesn’t worry at all about the disgrace
that will come to our family."
3419 vitta mā nitiyām matucūtaṉiyē alaṟṟi
kottu alar poḻil cūḻ kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ coṉṉa
pattu nūṟṟuḷ ip pattu avaṉ cēr tirukkōḷūrkkē
cittam vittu urippār tikaḻ poṉ ulaku āḷvārē (11)
3419. Saḍagopan of Thirukuruhur
surrounded by groves blooming with bunches of flowers
composed a thousand pasurams on the god Madhusudhanan,
the wonderful treasure saved and kept by all.
If devotees learn, keep in their minds
and recite these ten pasurams on Thirukoḷur
where the god stays
they will reach the golden world of moksha and rule there.
-------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (eṭṭām tirumoḻi, 3420-3430)
tirunāṭu mutaliyavaṟṟil talimakaḷ paṟavikaḷit tūtuviṭal
3420 poṉ ulaku āḷīrō puvaṉi muḻutu āḷīrō
nal nalap puḷḷiṉaṅkāḷ viṉiyāṭṭiyēṉ nāṉ irantēṉ
muṉ ulakaṅkaḷ ellām paṭitta mukilvaṇṇaṉ kaṇṇaṉ
eṉ nalam koṇṭa pirāṉ taṉakku eṉ nilimi urittē. (1)
3420. Her dughter says,
"O birds, you do good for others,
would you do me a favor?
I have done bad karma.
I ask one thing of you.
He created all the worlds.
The dark cloud-colored Kaṇṇan
has taken my happiness away.
Go and tell him how I suffer.
If you help me
you will reach moksha and rule the golden world there,
and you will rule this whole world also."
3421 mi amar vāḷ neṭum kaṇ maṅkimār muṉpu eṉ ki iruntu
ney amar iṉ aṭicil niccal pāloṭu mēvīrō
ki amar cakkarattu eṉ kaṉivāyp perumāṉik kaṇṭu
mey amar kātal colli kiḷikāḷ virintu ōṭivantē (2)
3421. Her dughter says,
"O parrots, fly quickly and go to see the highest lord.
His mouth is sweet as a fruit
and he carries a discus in his hand.
Go and tell him that my love is true.
If you do that I will keep you among my young friends
whose sharp long eyes are decorated with kohl
and feed you sweet rice mixed with ghee."
3422 ōṭivantu eṉ kuḻalmēl oḷi mā malar ūtīrō
kūṭiya vaṇṭiṉaṅkāḷ kurunāṭu uṭi aivarkaṭku āy
āṭiya mā neṭum tērppaṭi nīṟu eḻac ceṟṟa pirāṉ
cūṭiya taṇ tuḷavam uṇṭa tū matu vāykaḷ koṇṭē (3)
3422. Her dughter says,
"O bees, you fly together in a swarm!
The god stood on a great tall chariot on the battlefield
and destroyed his enemies for the five Pandavas,
the rulers of Kuruksetra.
Won’t you taste the shining beautiful flowers in my hir
after tasting the cool thulasi garland that the god wears
and drinking the honey from it?"
3423 tū matu vāykaḷ koṇṭuvantu eṉ mullikaḷmēl tumpikāḷ
pū matu uṇṇac cellil viṉiyēṉip poyceytu akaṉṟa
mā matu vār taṇ tuḻāy muṭi vāṉavar kōṉik kaṇṭu
yām ituvō takkavāṟu eṉṉavēṇṭum kaṇṭīr nuṅkaṭkē? (4)
3423. Her dughter says,
"O thumbi flies! You stay on my mulli flowers
and drink honey from them.
If you go to drink the honey on flowers,
see the king of the gods, adorned with a cool thulasi garland
that drips with sweet honey.
Surely, I have done bad karma.
He lied to me and left me.
Ask him, ‘Is what you do fair?
She loves you.
What do you want from her to love her back?’"
3424 nuṅkaṭku yāṉ urikkēṉ vammiṉ yāṉ vaḷartta kiḷikāḷ
vem kaṇ puḷ ūrntu vantu viṉiyēṉi neñcam kavarnta
ceṅkaṇ karumukili ceyya vāyc ceḻuṅ kaṟpakatti
eṅkuc ceṉṟākilum kaṇṭu ituvō takkavāṟu? eṉmiṉē (5)
3424. Her dughter says,
"O parrots, I rised you.
I want to say something to you. Come to me.
I have done bad karma.
The god that rides on cruel-eyed Garuḍa
saw me and attracted my heart.
If you see the lord who is generous as a karpaga tree,
with beautiful eyes, a color dark as a cloud and a red mouth,
ask him, ‘Is what you do fair?’"
3425 eṉ miṉṉu nūl mārvaṉ eṉ karum perumāṉ eṉ kaṇṇaṉ
taṉ maṉṉu nīḷ kaḻalmēl taṇ tuḻāy namakku aṉṟi nalkāṉ
kalmiṉkaḷ eṉṟu ummi yāṉ kaṟpiyāvitta māṟṟamcolli
celmiṉkaḷ tīviṉiyēṉ vaḷartta ciṟu pūvikaḷē (6)
3425. Her dughter says,
"O small puvi birds, I rised you.
I have done bad karma.
The highest dark-colored Kaṇṇan,
wears a shining sacred thread on his chest.
He will not give his cool thulasi garland
that adorns his long body to anyone but me.
Wherever you fly say the words that I have tught you.
When you see him ask him, ‘Is what you do fair?’"
3426 pūvikaḷ pōl niṟattaṉ puṇṭarīkaṅkaḷ pōlum kaṇṇaṉ
yāviyum yāvarum āy niṉṟa māyaṉ eṉ āḻip pirāṉ
māvi val vāy piḷanta matucūtaṟku eṉ māṟṟam colli
pāvikaḷ tīrkkiṟṟirē viṉiyāṭṭiyēṉ pācaṟavē? (7)
3425. Her dughter says,
"O small puvi birds, I rised you.
I have done bad karma.
The highest dark-colored Kaṇṇan,
wears a shining sacred thread on his chest.
He will not give his cool thulasi garland
that adorns his long body to anyone but me.
Wherever you fly say the words that I have tught you.
When you see him ask him, ‘Is what you do fair?’"
3427 pācaṟavu eyti iṉṉē viṉiyēṉ eṉi ūḻi nivēṉ
ācu aṟu tūvi veḷḷik kurukē aruḷceytu orunāḷ
mācu aṟu nīlac cuṭar muṭi vāṉavar kōṉik kaṇṭu
ēcu aṟum nummi allāl maṟunōkku ilaḷ pērttu maṟṟē (8)
3427. Her dughter says,
"O white kurugu bird with faultless white feathers,
have pity on me.
I no longer have any love for my relatives.
How many eons can I suffer?
Go and see the king of gods adorned with a shining crown
and a faultless dark color and tell him,
‘She doesn’t want to think of anyone but you, the faultless one.
She is waiting for you to give her your grace.’"
3428 pērttu maṟṟu ōr kaḷikaṇ viṉiyāṭṭiyēṉ nāṉ oṉṟu ilēṉ
nīrt tirimēl ulavi iri tērum putā iṉaṅkāḷ
kārt tiraḷ mā mukil pōl kaṇṇaṉ viṇṇavar kōṉik kaṇṭu
vārttikaḷ koṇṭu aruḷi uriyīr vikal vantiruntē (9)
3428. Her dughter says,
"I have done bad karma. I have no refuge but him.
O flock of cranes!
You search for food wandering near the rippling water.
Go and see the beautiful thick cloud-colored Kaṇṇan,
the king of the gods in the sky
and tell him of my love sickness.
Then come back at dawn and tell me what he told you."
3429 vantiruntu ummuṭiya maṇic cēvalum nīrum ellām
antaram oṉṟum iṉṟi alarmēl aciyum aṉṉaṅkāḷ
eṉ tiru mārvaṟku eṉṉi iṉṉavāṟu ivaḷ kāṇmiṉ eṉṟu
mantirattu oṉṟu uṇartti uriyīr maṟumāṟṟaṅkaḷē (10)
3429. Her dughter says,
"O swans, you stay happily on flowers with your beloved beautiful mates,
so close to them there is no space between you.
Go to my lord on whose chest lovely Lakshmi stays
and tell him, ‘Look at her condition. She suffers for the love of you.’
Repeat that as if it were a manthra so he will understand it.
Then come back in the morning and tell me his answer."
3430 māṟṟaṅkaḷ āyntukoṇṭu matucūta pirāṉ aṭimēl
nāṟṟaṅkoḷ pūm poḻil cūḻ kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ coṉṉa
tōṟṟaṅkaḷ āyirattuḷ iviyum orupattum vallār
ūṟṟiṉkaṇ nuṇ maṇal pōl urukāniṟpar nīrāyē (11)
3430. Sadagopan of Thirukuruhur
surrounded by fragrant blooming groves
composed a thousand pasurams describing how the girls
who fall in love with Madhusudanan
send their birds to him as messengers.
If devotees learn these ten pasurams and recite them
their hearts will melt for the god
like soft sand in a spring.
-------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (oṉpatām tirumoḻi, 3431-3441)
kēṭṭōr neñcam nīrāy urukumvaṇṇam āḻvār emperumāṉik kūppiṭutal
3431 nīr āy nilaṉ āy tī āy kāl āy neṭu vāṉ āy
cīr ār cuṭarkaḷ iraṇṭu āy civaṉ āy ayaṉ āṉāy
kūr ār āḻi veṇ caṅku ēnti koṭiyēṉpāl
vārāy orunāḷ maṇṇum viṇṇum makiḻavē (1)
3431. O lord, you are the ocean, earth, fire, wind and the wide sky,
the bright sun and the moon, Shiva and Brahma.
I have done bad karma.
One day please come to me carrying your sharp discus
and your white conch as the earth and sky exult in joy.
3432 maṇṇum viṇṇum makiḻa kuṟaḷ āy valam kāṭṭi
maṇṇum viṇṇum koṇṭa māya ammāṉē
naṇṇi uṉi nāṉ kaṇṭu ukantu kūttāṭa
naṇṇi orunāḷ ñālattūṭē naṭavāyē (2)
3432. You, a magical one, are my mother.
You took the form of a dwarf
and took the earth and sky at Mahabali’s sacrifice.
Come to this earth one day
and I will approach you, see you and dance happily.
3433 ñālattūṭē naṭantum niṉṟum kiṭantu iruntum
cālap pala nāḷ ukamtōṟu uyirkaḷ kāppāṉē
kōlat tiru mā makaḷōṭu uṉṉik kūṭātē
cālap pala nāḷ aṭiyēṉ iṉṉum taḷarvēṉō? (3)
3433. You come to this earth.
walk, stand and sleep and protect the creatures of the world,
staying here happily.
I want to see you with the beautiful divine Lakshmi.
I, your devotee, have grown tired waiting so long to see you.
3434 taḷarntum muṟintum cakaṭa acurar uṭal vēṟāp
piḷantu vīya tirukkāl āṇṭa perumāṉē
kiḷarntu piramaṉ civaṉ intiraṉ viṇṇavar cūḻa
viḷaṅka orunāḷ kāṇa vārāy viṇmītē (4)
3434. You are the highest god.
Sakatasuran came as a cart
and with your legs you kicked and killed him,
breaking the cart into pieces
so the whole world would know your power.
Show yourself in the sky one day,
surrounded by Brahma, Shiva, Indra and all the gods,
so the whole world can see you.
3435 viṇmītu iruppāy malimēl niṟpāy kaṭal cērppāy
maṇmītu uḻalvāy ivaṟṟuḷ eṅkum maṟintu uṟivāy
eṇmītu iyaṉṟa puṟa aṇṭattāy eṉatu āvi
yuḷ mītu āṭi uruk kāṭṭātē oḷippāyō? (5)
3435. You stay in the sky.
You stay in a standing form on Thiruvenkaṭam hills.
You sleep on the milky ocean.
You came to the earth in many forms.
You abide, hiding yourself.
You are above our thoughts and stay above the earth.
You are in my soul and you stay there
but you do not show your form to my eyes.
3436 pāy ōr aṭi vittu ataṉ kīḻp paravi nilam ellām
tāy ōr aṭiyāl ellā ulakum taṭavanta
māyōṉ uṉṉik kāṇpāṉ varunti eṉināḷum
tīyōṭu uṭaṉcēr meḻukāy ulakil tirivēṉō? (6)
3436. You put one foot on this earth
and took over all the land.
You are Maayon who took the whole earth at Mahabali’s sacrifice.
I melt like wax in fire,
suffering to see you and wandering in the world.
3437 ulakil tiriyum karuma kati āy ulakam āy
ulakukkē ōr uyirum āṉāy puṟa aṇṭattu
alakil polinta tici pattu āya aruvēyō
alakil polinta aṟivilēṉukku aruḷāyē (7)
3437. You are all the actions of the world.
You are the refuge for all creatures of the world.
You are the world.
You are the soul on this earth.
You have no form
but you shine in all the ten directions.
I am ignorant. Give me your grace, O shining lord.
3438 aṟivilēṉukku aruḷāy aṟivār uyir āṉāy
veṟi koḷ cōti mūrtti aṭiyēṉ neṭumālē
kiṟiceytu eṉṉip puṟattiṭṭu iṉṉam keṭuppāyō
piṟitu oṉṟu aṟiyā aṭiyēṉ āvi tikikkavē? (8)
3438. I am ignorant. Give me your grace.
You are my wise soul and you shine.
O Neḍumaal, I am your slave.
Are you playing with me, cheating me and spoiling me?
I am your slave.
You shock my heart with your games
but I know nothing else but loving you.
3439 āvi tikikka aivar kumikkum ciṟṟiṉpam
pāviyēṉip pala nī kāṭṭip paṭuppāyō?
tāvi viyam koṇṭa taṭam tāmari kaṭkē
kūvik koḷḷum kālam iṉṉam kuṟukātō? (9)
3439. All my five senses hurt and shock my soul.
I am a sinner.
Why do you play with me so much and make me suffer?
You have measured the world
with your beautiful lotus feet that grew into the sky.
Won’t the time come soon when I can call you and join you?
3440 kuṟukā nīḷā iṟutikūṭā eṉi ūḻi
ciṟukā perukā aḷavu il iṉpam cērntālum
maṟu kāl iṉṟi māyōṉ uṉakkē āḷākum
ciṟu kālatti uṟumō antō teriyilē? (10)
3440. Pleasures in the world may shrink or grow,
they may go away.
The world may end and agin arise.
O Maayon, even if I join you for a little time
that is much better than all the happiness I get in this world.
I do not know when that time will come.
3441 terital niṉital eṇṇal ākāt tirumālukku
uriya toṇṭar toṇṭar toṇṭaṉ caṭakōpaṉ
teriyac coṉṉa ōr āyirattuḷ ip pattum
uriya toṇṭar ākkum ulakam uṇṭāṟkē (11)
3441. Saḍagopan, the devotee of the devotees
of the devotees of dear Thirumaal
who know and think only of the god
composed a thousand good poems.
If devotees learn and recite these ten poems
they will become the devotees of the god of the world.
-------------
tiruvāymoḻi - āṟām pattu (pattām tirumoḻi, 3442-3452)
tiruvēṅkaṭamuṭiyāṉatu tiruvaṭikaḷil caraṇampukutal (tiruvēṅkaṭam)
3442 ulakam uṇṭa peruvāyā
ulappu il kīrtti ammāṉē
nilavum cuṭar cūḻ oḷi mūrtti
neṭiyāy aṭiyēṉ ār uyirē
tilatam ulakukku āy niṉṟa
tiruvēṅkaṭattu em perumāṉē
kula tol aṭiyēṉ uṉa pātam
kūṭum āṟu kūṟāyē (1)
3442. O god,
you swallowed all the worlds with your huge mouth.
O father, you have limitless fame.
You shine surrounded by the sun and moon.
You are the tall god.
You are dear life for me, your slave.
You are the great god of Thiruvenkaṭam hill
and you are like a thilakam for the world.
I am your devotee born in an ancient family.
Tell me how to reach your feet.
3443 kūṟu āy nīṟu āy nilaṉ āki
koṭu val acurar kulam ellām
cīṟā eriyum tiru nēmi
valavā teyvak kōmāṉē
cēṟu ār cuṉit tāmari centī
malarum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
āṟā aṉpil aṭiyēṉ uṉ
aṭicēr vaṇṇam aruḷāyē (2)
3443. You are the form of the five elements
fire, land, water. fire and wind.
You carry a divine discus
and you killed all the strong cruel Rakshasa tribes
and burned their lands.
You are the lord of Thiruvenkaṭam
where lotuses red as fire bloom from the mud in springs.
I love you without any limit.
Give me your grace and bring me to your feet.
3444 vaṇṇam maruḷ koḷ aṇi mēka
vaṇṇā māya ammāṉē
eṇṇam pukuntu tittikkum
amutē imiyōr atipatiyē
teḷ nal aruvi maṇi poṉ muttu
alikkum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
aṇṇalē uṉ aṭi cēra
aṭiyēṟku āā eṉṉāyē (3)
3444. O my father,
you, beautiful cloud-colored Maayan, enchant everyone.
You are sweet nectar and you enter my thoughts.
You are the lord of the gods whose eyes do not blink.
You are the god of Thiruvenkaṭam
where a clear waterfalls descends beautifully
scattering diamonds, gold and pearls.
You are the highest.
Give me your grace and bring me to your feet.
3445 āā eṉṉātu ulakatti
alikkum acurar vāḻ nāḷmēl
tī vāy vāḷi maḻi poḻinta
ciliyā tiru mā makaḷ kēḷvā
tēvā curarkaḷ muṉikkaṇaṅkaḷ
virumpum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
pū ār kaḻalkaḷ aruviṉiyēṉ
poruntumāṟu puṇarāyē (4)
3445. The Asurans troubled people
without feeling sorry for them
and you shot your fiery arrows like rin and killed them.
O divine god, you are the beloved of Lakshmi.
You are the lord of Thiruvenkaṭam
where sages and the gods in the sky love to worship you.
I have done bad karma.
Give me your grace and bring me to your lotus feet
ornamented with anklets.
3446 puṇarā niṉṟa maram ēḻ aṉṟu
eyta oru vil valavā ō
puṇar ēy niṉṟa maram iraṇṭiṉ
naṭuvē pōṉa mutalvā ō
tiṇar ār mēkam eṉak kaḷiṟu
cērum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
tiṇar ār cārṅkattu uṉa pātam
cērvatu aṭiyēṉ ennāḷē? (5)
3446. You are the mighty one who, shooting one arrow,
destroyed the seven trees that were joined together.
You, the ancient one, went between the Asurans
who came as two trees.
You stay in Thiruvenkaṭam where a herd of strong elephants
looks like a group of clouds.
You carry the mighty bow Charngam.
When will I, your devotee, truly join your feet?
3447 ennāḷē nām maṇ aḷanta
iṇit tāmarikaḷ kāṇpataṟku eṉṟu
ennāḷum niṉṟu imiyōrkaḷ
ētti iṟiñci iṉam iṉamāy
meyn nā maṉattāl vaḻipāṭu
ceyyum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
meyn nāṉ eyti en nāḷ uṉ
aṭikkaṇ aṭiyēṉ mēvuvatē? (6)
3447. You are the god of Thiruvenkaṭam
where the gods in the sky come, prise and worship
with their bodies, tongues and minds, saying,
"When will the day come when we see
the lotus feet that measured the world?"
When will the true day come
when I, your devotee, come and join your feet?
3448 aṭiyēṉ mēvi amarkiṉṟa
amutē imiyōr atipatiyē
koṭiyā aṭu puḷ uṭiyāṉē
kōlak kaṉivāyp perumāṉē
ceṭi ār viṉikaḷ tīr maruntē
tiruvēṅkaṭattu em perumāṉē
noṭi ār poḻutum uṉa pātam
kāṇa nōlātu āṟṟēṉē (7)
3448. You are my nectar.
and I, your devotee, enjoy worshipping you.
You are the lord of the gods and you carry an eagle flag.
O highest one, your beautiful mouth is as sweet as a fruit.
You are the remedy for cruel karma.
You are the god of Thiruvenkaṭam.
I suffer wanting to see your feet every moment
and I cannot bear it.
3449 nōlātu āṟṟēṉ uṉa pātam
kāṇa eṉṟu nuṇ uṇarviṉ
nīl ār kaṇṭattu ammāṉum
niṟi nāṉmukaṉum intiraṉum
cēl ēy kaṇṇār palar cūḻa
virumpum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
mālāy mayakki aṭiyēṉpāl
vantāy pōlē vārāyē (8)
3449. Shiva who has a dark neck,
faultless Nanmuhan, Indra
and beautiful fish-eyed women
join together in Thiruvenkaṭam
and worship you, saying "We cannot wait to see your feet!"
You came to the earth as Maal and fascinated everyone.
Won’t you come to us
just as you have always come to the world?
3450 vantāy pōlē vārātāy
vārātāy pōl varuvāṉē
centāmarik kaṇ ceṅkaṉi vāy
nāl tōḷ amutē eṉatu uyirē
cintāmaṇikaḷ pakar allip
pakal cey tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
antō aṭiyēṉ uṉa pātam
akalakillēṉ iṟiyumē (9)
3450. It seems as if you are coming,
but you do not come to me.
It seems you are not coming, but you do come to me.
You have lovely lotus eyes
and a red mouth like sweet fruit or nectar.
You are my life.
You are the god of Thiruvenkaṭam
where chintamaṇi jewels turn night into day.
Alas, I am your slave.
I cannot be away from your feet even for a moment.
3451 akalakillēṉ iṟiyum eṉṟu
alarmēl maṅki uṟi mārpā
nikar il pukaḻāy ulakam mūṉṟu
uṭiyāy eṉṉi āḷvāṉē
nikar il amarar muṉikkaṇaṅkaḷ
virumpum tiruvēṅkaṭattāṉē
pukal oṉṟu illā aṭiyēṉ uṉ
aṭikkīḻ amarntu pukuntēṉē. (10)
3451. Lakshmi on a lotus says,
"I will not go away from you even for a moment"
and stays on your chest.
Nothing compares to your fame.
You rule all three worlds and you are my king.
You are the lord of Thiruvenkaṭam
whom the matchless gods and sages love.
I have no other refuge.
I am your slave and I have come to you to sit beneath your feet.
3452 aṭikkīḻ amarntu pukuntu aṭiyīr
vāḻmiṉ eṉṟu eṉṟu aruḷkoṭukkum
paṭik kēḻ illāp perumāṉip
paḻaṉak kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ
muṭippāṉ coṉṉa āyirattut
tiruvēṅkaṭattukku ivi pattum
piṭittār piṭittār vīṟṟiruntu
periya vāṉuḷ nilāvuvarē. (11)
3452. Sadagopan of Thirukuruhur flourishing with paddy fields
composed a thousand pasurams on the matchless lord
of Thiruvenkaṭam who gives his grace, saying,
"Come and sit beneath my feet and you will be saved."
If devotees learn and recite these ten pasurams
they will become the devotees of his devotees
and reach and abide in moksha in the wide sky.
-------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

nammāḻvār tiruvāymoḻi eḻām pattu (3453 - 3562) Divyaprabhandam - Thiruvaymozhi - Eezhaam Pathu 3453 - 3562

tiruvāymoḻi eḻām pattu (mutal tirumoḻi, 3453-3463)
intiriyaṅkaḷāl iṉṉum ettaṉi nāḷ tuṉpuṟuvēṉ eṉṟu āḻvār varuntal
3453 uḷ nilāviya aivarāl kumitīṟṟi eṉṉi
uṉ pātapaṅkayam
naṇṇilāvakiyē nalivāṉ iṉṉum eṇṇukiṉṟāy
eṇ ilāp peṟu māyaṉē imiyōrkaḷ
ēttum ulakam mūṉṟu uṭi
aṇṇalē amutē appaṉē eṉṉi āḷvāṉē (1)
3453. You are still intent on giving my body
the feelings of the five senses even though they hurt me
and do not let me reach your lotus feet.
You are the great Maayan—no one knows who you are.
You are the ruler, prised by the gods, of the three worlds.
You are nectar, my father, and you rule me.
3454 eṉṉi āḷum vaṉ kō ōr aintu ivi peytu
irāppakal mōtuvittiṭṭu
uṉṉi nāṉ aṇukāvaki ceytu pōtikaṇṭāy
kaṉṉalē amutē kār mukil vaṇṇaṉē
kaṭal ñālam kākkiṉṟa
miṉṉu nēmiyiṉāy viṉiyēṉuṭi vētiyaṉē (2)
3454. You make the perceptions of my five bodily senses rule,
hurting me night and day and attacking my goodness
so I am unable to come to you.
You are as sweet as sugarcane juice and divine nectar.
You have the color of a dark cloud.
You carry a shining discus that protects the world
surrounded by oceans.
You are the lord of the Vedas and you gave me my karma.
3455 vētiyāniṟkum aivarāl viṉiyēṉi
mōtuvittu uṉ tiruvaṭic
cātiyāvaki nī taṭuttu eṉ peṟuti? antō!
āti āki akal iṭam paṭittu uṇṭu
umiḻntu kaṭantu iṭantiṭṭa
cōti nīḷ muṭiyāy toṇṭaṉēṉ matucūtaṉaṉē (3)
3455. What do you get
by making the perceptions of the five senses hurt me
and increasing my bad karma,
preventing me from reaching your divine feet?
Alas, you are the ancient god.
You created this wide world, swallowed it and spit it out.
You, the shining lord took the form of a boar,
split open the ground and brought up earth goddess
when an Asuran took and hid her.
You are decorated with a shining crown.
I am your devotee.
You are Madhusudanan.
3456 cūtu nāṉ aṟiyāvaki cuḻaṟṟi ōr aivarik
kāṭṭi uṉ aṭip
pōtu nāṉ aṇukāvaki ceytu pōtikaṇṭāy
yātum yāvarum iṉṟi niṉ akampāl oṭukki
ōr āliṉ nīḷ ili
mītu cēr kuḻavi viṉiyēṉ viṉitīr maruntē (4)
3456. See, you made the perceptions of the five senses hurt me
and I could not reach your flower-like feet.
You contin all things and creatures of the world
in your stomach and sleep on a banyan leaf as a child.
I have done bad karma.
You are the remedy that can relieve me of my karma.
3457 tīr maruntu iṉṟi aintu nōy aṭum cekkil iṭṭut
tirikkum aivari
nēr maruṅku uṭittā aṭittu nekiḻppāṉ okkiṉṟāy
ār maruntu iṉi ākuvār aṭal āḻi ēnti
acurar vaṉ kulam
vēr maruṅku aṟuttāy viṇṇuḷār perumāṉē? ō (5)
3457. You make the five senses hurt me and I do not know
any remedy to counter the pin they give me.
Who can relieve me of that pin?
You destroyed the terrible tribe of the Asurans with your heroic discus.
You are the god of the gods in the sky.
3458 viṇṇuḷār perumāṟku aṭimi ceyvāriyum
ceṟum aimpulaṉ ivi
maṇṇuḷ eṉṉip peṟṟāl eṉ ceyyā maṟṟu nīyum viṭṭāl?
paṇṇuḷāy kavi taṉṉuḷāy pattiyiṉ uḷḷāy
paramīcaṉē vantu eṉ
kaṇṇuḷāy neñcuḷāy colluḷāy oṉṟu collāyē (6)
3458. The five senses hurt even the devotees of the lord.
I just live in this world—how could they not hurt me?
You are in music, you are in poetry,
you are the devotion of devotees.
You, the highest god,
come and stay in my heart and in my eyes.
Speak to me and tell me something
to get rid of the feelings that the five senses give me.
3459 oṉṟu colli oruttiṉil niṟkilāta
ōr aivar vaṉ kayavari
eṉṟu yāṉ velkiṟpaṉ uṉ tiruvaruḷ illiyēl?
aṉṟu tēvar acurar vāṅka alikaṭal
aravam aḷāvi ōr
kuṉṟam vitta entāy koṭiyēṉ paruku iṉ amutē (7)
3459. Unless you give me your grace,
how can I conquer the five senses,
the thieves that make me unstable?
O father, when the gods could not churn
the milky ocean with its roaring waves
you helped them and churned it with them
using the snake Vasuki and Manthara mountin.
I am cruel, and you are nectar that I can drink.
3460 iṉ amutu eṉat tōṉṟi ōr aivar yāvariyum
mayakka nī vitta
muṉṉam māyam ellām muḻu vēr arintu eṉṉi uṉ
ciṉṉamum tiru mūrttiyum cintittu ēttik
kitoḻavē aruḷ eṉakku
eṉ ammā eṉ kaṇṇā imiyōr tam kulamutalē (8)
3460. Before, you gave me the pleasures of the five senses
and fascinated me with your magic.
Now you come as sweet nectar and utterly remove
all the joy of my senses.
Give me your grace to worship your form
and your discus, conch and club.
You are the three gods, Shiva, Brahma and Indra.
You are my mother, my dear one,
and the source of the gods in the sky.
3461 kulam mutal aṭum tīviṉik koṭu vaṉ kuḻiyiṉil
vīḻkkum aivari
valam mutal keṭukkum varamē tantaruḷkaṇṭāy
nilam mutal iṉi ev ulakukkum niṟpaṉa
celvaṉa eṉap poruḷ
pala mutal paṭittāy eṉ kaṇṇā eṉ parañcuṭarē (9)
3461. The pleasures of the five senses that give bad karma
will destroy everyone in someone’s family.
You gave me a boon and destroyed those pleasures.
You created all the worlds and the creatures
that stay and move and all other things.
You are my Kaṇṇan. You are the highest light.
3462 eṉ parañcuṭarē eṉṟu uṉṉi alaṟṟi
uṉ iṇit tāmarikaṭku
aṉpu uruki niṟkum atu niṟka cumaṭu tantāy
vaṉ paraṅkaḷ eṭuttu aivar tici tici
valittu eṟṟukiṉṟaṉar
muṉ paravi kaṭintu amutam koṇṭa mūrtti ō (10)
3462. You gave me the burden
of melting in love for you and worshiping your lotus feet,
crying and saying, "You are the highest light!"
The five senses stand everywhere and threaten me.
O lord, you churned the milky ocean and took nectar from it.
3463 koṇṭa mūrtti ōr mūvarāyk kuṇaṅkaḷ paṭittu
aḷittu keṭukkum ap
puṇṭarīkak koppūḻp puṉal paḷḷi appaṉukkē
toṇṭar toṇṭar toṇṭar toṇṭaṉ
caṭakōpaṉ col āyirattuḷ ip pattum
kaṇṭu pāṭa vallār viṉi pōm kaṅkulum pakalē (11)
3463. Saḍagopan, a devotee of the devotees of the devotees
of the devotees of the lord composed a thousand pasurams on the god.
Brahma stays on the lotus on his navel.
He, our father, has three forms, Shiva, Brahma and Indra.
He creates the world, protects it and destroys it.
If devotees learn and recite these ten pasurams
they will have no results of karma, day or night.
-----------
tiruvāymoḻi eḻām pattu (iraṇṭām tirumoḻi, 3464-3475)
tiruvaraṅkap perumāṉiṭam talivi mikavum mōkittiruppatik
kaṇṭa tāy araṅkarip pārttu viṉāvutal (tiruvaraṅkam)
3464 kaṅkulum pakalum kaṇ tuyil aṟiyāḷ
kaṇṇa nīr kikaḷāl iṟikkum
caṅku cakkaraṅkaḷ eṉṟu ki kūppum
tāmarik kaṇ eṉṟē taḷarum
eṅṅaṉē tarikkēṉ uṉṉiviṭṭu? eṉṉum
iru nilam ki tuḻā irukkum
ceṅkayal pāy nīrt tiruvaraṅkattāy
ivaḷ tiṟattu eṉ ceykiṉṟāyē? (1)
3464. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter doesn’t sleep night or day.
With her fingers, she wipes away the tears that drip from her eyes.
She folds her hands, worships and says, "Conch and discus!"
Tired, she says, "He has lotus eyes!"
She says, "How can I survive without you?"
and with her hands, she searches all over for you.
You are the god of Thiruvarangam
abundant with water where lovely kayal fish frolic.
What did you do to make her like this?"
3465 eṉ ceykiṉṟāy eṉ tāmarik kaṇṇā?
eṉṉum kaṇṇīr malka irukkum
eṉ ceykēṉ eṟi nīrt tiruvaraṅkattāy?
eṉṉum vevvuyirttu uyirttu urukum
muṉ ceyta viṉiyē mukappaṭāy eṉṉum
mukilvaṇṇā takuvatō? eṉṉum
muṉ ceytu iv ulakam uṇṭu umiḻntu aḷantāy
eṉkolō muṭikiṉṟatu ivaṭkē? (2)
3465. Her mother worries and says,
" My dughter says,
‘You are my lotus-eyed Kaṇṇan. Why do you do this to me?’
Her eyes fill with tears and she says,
‘You are the lord of Thiruvarangam
surrounded by the ocean roaring with waves.
What will I do?’ and she sighs and melts.
She says, ‘I have done much bad karma
and that is why you don’t come before me.
Is that right?’
You swallowed the world and spit it out.
What will happen to her?"
3466 vaṭku ilaḷ iṟiyum maṇivaṇṇā eṉṉum
vāṉamē nōkkum miyākkum
uṭku uṭi acurar uyir ellām uṇṭa
oruvaṉē eṉṉum uḷ urukum
kaṭkilī uṉṉik kāṇumāṟu aruḷāy
kākuttā kaṇṇaṉē eṉṉum
tiṇ koṭi matiḷ cūḻ tiruvaraṅkattāy
ivaḷtiṟattu eṉ ceytiṭṭāyē? (3)
3466. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter doesn’t feel ashamed at all.
She says, ‘You have the color of blue sapphire.’
She looks at the sky and, fascinated, and she says,
‘The Asurans gave fear to all people.
You are unique and you destroyed them.’
Her heart melts.
She says, ‘No one can see you.
Give me your grace so I may see you.
You are Raman of the Kahusta dynasty.
You are Kaṇṇan.’
You stay in Thiruvarangam
surrounded by walls where flags fly.
What did you do to fascinate her?"
3467 iṭṭa kāl iṭṭa kiyaḷāy irukkum
eḻuntu ulāy mayaṅkum ki kūppum
kaṭṭamē kātal eṉṟu mūrccikkum
kaṭalvaṇṇā kaṭiyikāṇ eṉṉum
vaṭṭa vāy nēmi valaṅkiyā eṉṉum
vantiṭāy eṉṟu eṉṟē mayaṅkum
ciṭṭaṉē ceḻu nīrt tiruvaraṅkattāy
ivaḷtiṟattu eṉ cintittāyē? (4)
3467. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter stays in one place
without moving her hands and legs.
Sometime she gets up and walks around.
She is fascinated with someone.
She folds her hands and worships.
She says, ‘Love is trouble’ and sighs.
In her delusion, she says, ‘You have the color of the ocean.
See, you are difficult.
You carry a round discus in your left hand. Come to me.’
You are a chithan. You stay in Thiruvarangam
surrounded by flourishing water.
What do you think you are doing to her?"
3468 cintikkum ticikkum tēṟum ki kūppum
tiruvaraṅkattuḷḷāy eṉṉum
vantikkum āṅkē maḻikkaṇ nīr malka
vantiṭāy eṉṟu eṉṟē mayaṅkum
antippōtu avuṇaṉ uṭal iṭantāṉē
ali kaṭal kaṭinta ār amutē
cantittu uṉ caraṇam cārvatē valitta
tiyali miyal ceytāṉē (5)
3468. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter thinks deeply.
She folds her hands and worships all the directions.
She says, ‘You stay in Thiruvarangam,’
Her eyes fill with tears and she worships you.
She says, ‘Come to me’ and is fascinated with you.
She says, ‘Once, in the evening,
you split open the body of Hiranyan.
You are the sweet nectar that was churned
from the milky ocean rolling with waves.’
She thinks only you are her refuge and lives for you.
You have made her fall in love with you."
3469 miyal ceytu eṉṉi maṉam kavarntāṉē
eṉṉum mā māyaṉē eṉṉum
ceyya vāy maṇiyē eṉṉum taṇ puṉal cūḻ
tiruvaraṅkattuḷḷāy eṉṉum
veyya vāḷ taṇṭu caṅku cakkaram vil
ēntum viṇṇōr mutal eṉṉum;
pi koḷ pāmpu aṇiyāy ivaḷtiṟattu aruḷāy
pāviyēṉ ceyaṟpālatuvē (6)
3469. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter says,
‘He fascinated me and attracted my mind.’
She says, ‘You are a real Maayan.’
She says, ‘You are a diamond and your red mouth is beautiful.’
She says, ‘You stay in Thiruvarangam surrounded by cool water.’
She says, ‘You are the ancient god of gods in the sky
and you carry a strong sword, club, conch, discus and a bow.’
O god, you sleep on Adisesha. Give her your grace.
This is the only thing I ask you."
3470 pāla tuṉpaṅkaḷ iṉpaṅkaḷ paṭittāy
paṟṟilār paṟṟa niṉṟāṉē
kāla cakkarattāy kaṭal iṭam koṇṭa
kaṭalvaṇṇā kaṇṇaṉē eṉṉum
cēl koḷ taṇ puṉal cūḻ tiruvaraṅkattāy
eṉṉum eṉ tīrttaṉē eṉṉum
kōla mā maḻikkaṇ paṉi malka irukkum
eṉṉuṭik kōmaḷak koḻuntē (7)
3470. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter says,
‘You created sorrows and happiness.
You are desire for those who have no desire.
You carry the discus that destroys those who are evil.
You have the color of the ocean and you sleep on the ocean.
You are Kannan.’
She says, ‘You stay in Thiruvarangam
surrounded by water where fish frolic.’
She says, ‘You are my sacred temple.’
She is my lovely dughter, soft as a tender shoot
and her beautiful eyes are filled with tears."
3471 koḻuntu vāṉavarkaṭku eṉṉum kuṉṟu ēnti
kō niri kāttavaṉ eṉṉum
aḻum toḻum āvi aṉala vevvuyirkkum
añcaṉa vaṇṇaṉē eṉṉum
eḻuntu mēl nōkki imippilaḷ irukkum
eṅṅaṉē nōkkukēṉ? eṉṉum
ceḻum taṭam puṉal cūḻ tiruvaraṅkattāy
eṉ ceykēṉ eṉ tirumakaṭkē? (8)
3471. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter says,
‘He is a tender shoot for the gods in the sky.’
She says, ‘He carried Govardhana mountin
and protected the cows.’
She cries for him and worships him
and her sighs are hot as fire.
She says, ‘You have the color of kohl.’
She just looks up and doesn’t even blink her eyes.
She says, ‘Where should I look to find you?’
You are the lord of Thiruvarangam
surrounded by flourishing ponds filled with water.
What should I do for my lovely dughter?"
3472 eṉ tirumakaḷ cēr mārvaṉē eṉṉum
eṉṉuṭi āviyē eṉṉum
niṉ tiru eyiṟṟāl iṭantu nī koṇṭa
nilamakaḷ kēḷvaṉē eṉṉum
aṉṟu uru ēḻum taḻuvi nī koṇṭa
āymakaḷ aṉpaṉē eṉṉum
teṉ tiruvaraṅkam kōyilkoṇṭāṉē
teḷikilēṉ muṭivu ivaḷ taṉakkē (9)
3472. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter says,
‘You embrace lovely Lakshmi on your chest.’
She says, ‘You are my life.’
She says, ‘You are the beloved of the earth goddess
whom you brought out from the underworld
taking the form of a boar and splitting open the earth.’
She says, ‘You conquered seven bulls
to marry the cowherd girl Nappinni
and you are her beloved husband.’
You stay in the temple of south Thiruvarangam.
I don’t understand what will happen to my dughter."
3473 muṭivu ivaḷ taṉakku oṉṟu aṟikilēṉ eṉṉum
mūvulaku āḷiyē eṉṉum
kaṭi kamaḻ koṉṟic caṭiyaṉē eṉṉum
nāṉmukak kaṭavuḷē eṉṉum
vaṭivu uṭi vāṉōr talivaṉē eṉṉum
vaṇ tiruvaraṅkaṉē eṉṉum
aṭi aṭiyātāḷ pōl ivaḷ aṇuki
aṭintaṉaḷ mukilvaṇṇaṉ aṭiyē (10)
3473. Her mother worries and says,
"My dughter says,
‘I don’t see any end to my suffering.’
She says, ‘You are the ruler of all the three worlds.’
She says, ‘You are Shiva, with a jata adorned with fragrant kondri
flowers.’
She says, ‘You are the god Nanmuhan.’
She says, ‘You are the god of the beautiful the gods in the sky.’
She says, ‘You stay in flourishing Thiruvarangam.’
She was not able to approach his feet
but now she attins the feet of the cloud-colored god."
3474 mukilvaṇṇaṉ aṭiyi aṭintu aruḷ cūṭi
uyntavaṉ moy puṉal porunal
tukil vaṇṇat tū nīrc cērppaṉ vaṇ poḻil cūḻ
vaṇ kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ
mukilvaṇṇaṉ aṭimēl coṉṉa col māli
āyirattu ip pattum vallār
mukil vaṇṇa vāṉattu imiyavar cūḻa
iruppar pēriṉpa veḷḷattē (11)
3474. Sadagopan of Thirukuruhur on the bank of a river
with abundant blue water surrounded by flourishing groves
composed a garland of a thousand pasurams
on the feet of the cloud-colored god.
If devotees learn these ten poems and recite them
they will reach the blue sky with clouds
and stay with the god in a flood of bliss.
-------------
tiruvāymoḻi eḻām pattu (mūṉṟām tirumoḻi, 3475-3485)
tāymārum tōḻimārum uṟṟārum taṭukkavum talivi
tiruppērēyil cērat tuṇital (tiruppēreyil)
3475 veḷḷic curi caṅkoṭu āḻi ēnti
tāmarik kaṇṇaṉ eṉ neñciṉūṭē
puḷḷik kaṭākiṉṟa āṟṟik kāṇīr
eṉ collic collukēṉ aṉṉimīrkāḷ?
veḷḷac cukam avaṉ vīṟṟirunta
vēta oliyum viḻā oliyum
piḷḷik kuḻā viḷiyāṭṭu oliyum
aṟā tiruppēreyil cērvaṉ nāṉē (1)
3475. Her dughter says,
"O Mothers, the lotus-eyed god Kaṇṇan
carrying a curved white conch and a discus
entered my heart riding on Garuḍa.
How can I describe how it was?
The sound of the parrots, the divine sound of the Vedas,
the loud noise of festivals
and the sound of children’s play
never stop in Thirupereyil.
When will I go and join that place
where the god stays giving grace like a flood."
3476 nāṉak karuṅ kuḻal tōḻimīrkāḷ
aṉṉiyarkāḷ ayal cēriyīrkāḷ
nāṉ it taṉi neñcam kākkamāṭṭēṉ
eṉ vacam aṉṟu itu irāppakal pōy
tēṉ moytta pūmpoḻil taṇ paṇi cūḻ
teṉ tiruppēreyil vīṟṟirunta
vāṉap pirāṉ maṇivaṇṇaṉ kaṇṇaṉ
ceṅkaṉi vāyiṉ tiṟattatuvē (2)
3476. She says,
"O my friends, your hir is dark and fragrant.
O my mothers, O people of the village,
I cannot keep my heart with me.
It has gone from me to Kaṇṇan
and stays with him night and day.
I cannot bear being alone without my heart.
He stays in southern Thiruppereyil
filled with cool fields and blooming groves swarming with bees.
My heart has fallen for the beauty of the sweet fruit-like mouth
of the sapphire-colored lord of the gods in the sky."
3477 ceṅkaṉi vāyiṉ tiṟattatāyum
ceñ cuṭar nīḷ muṭi tāḻntatāyum
caṅkoṭu cakkaram kaṇṭu ukantum
tāmarik kaṇkaḷukku aṟṟut tīrntum
tiṅkaḷum nāḷum viḻā aṟāta
teṉ tiruppēreyil vīṟṟirunta
naṅkaḷ pirāṉukku eṉ neñcam tōḻī
nāṇum niṟiyum iḻantatuvē (3)
3477. She says,
"O my friends! My heart rejoices.
It has fallen for the beauty of his sweet fruit-like mouth,
his long shining hir, his conch and discus and his lotus eyes.
He stays in southern Thiruperur
where festivals go on every day of every month.
My heart has lost its modesty and chastity for our lord."
3478 iḻanta em māmittiṟattup pōṉa
eṉ neñciṉārum aṅkē oḻintār
uḻantu iṉi yārikkoṇṭu eṉ ucākō?
ōtak kaṭal oli pōla eṅkum
eḻunta nal vētattu oli niṉṟu ōṅku
teṉ tiruppēreyil vīṟṟirunta
muḻaṅku caṅkak kiyaṉ māyattu āḻntēṉ
aṉṉiyarkāḷ eṉṉi eṉ muṉintē? (4)
3478. She says,
"O mothers,
my heart went to him to bring back the dark color that I lost,
but it stays there with him.
Who can I send to find out what has happened to it?
The lord carrying the sounding conch in his hands
stays in southern Thirupperiyil
where the recitation the good Vedas spreads everywhere
like the roaring sound of the ocean.
I fell into his maayam.
O mothers, what is the use of getting mad at me?"
3479 muṉintu cakaṭam utittu māyap
pēy muli uṇṭu marutu iṭi pōy
kaṉinta viḷavukkuk kaṉṟu eṟinta
kaṇṇa pirāṉukku eṉ peṇmi tōṟṟēṉ
muṉintu iṉi eṉ ceytīr aṉṉimīrkāḷ?
muṉṉi avaṉ vantu vīṟṟirunta
kaṉinta poḻil tiruppēreyiṟkē
kālampeṟa eṉṉik kāṭṭumiṉē (5)
3479. She says,
"O mothers,
Kaṇṇan grew angry at Sakatasuran
when he came in the form of a cart,
kicked him and destroyed the cart.
He drank milk from the breasts of the devil Putana.
When two Raksasas came as marudu trees,
he went in between them and destroyed them.
Other Raksasas that came as calves
were thrown by the lord at a Vilam tree and were killed.
O mothers, I have lost my chastity to him.
You are angry at me—what will you do?
He stays in Thiruppereyil where sweet fruits ripen in the groves.
Take me there and leave me.
Don’t wait."
3480 kālampeṟa eṉṉik kāṭṭumiṉkaḷ
kātal kaṭaliṉ mikap peritāl
nīla mukil vaṇṇattu em perumāṉ
niṟkum muṉṉē vantu eṉ kikkum eytāṉ
ñālattu avaṉ vantu vīṟṟirunta
nāṉmaṟiyāḷarum vēḷvi ōvā
kōlac cenneṟkaḷ kavari vīcum
kūṭu puṉal tiruppēreyiṟkē. (6)
3480. Her dughter says,
" O mothers! Love is larger than the ocean.
The dark cloud-colored god came to me
but I couldn’t catch him who was so dear with my hands.
Don’t wait too long.
Take me to Thiruppereyil surrounded by water
where the ruler of the world stays
and the sound of the Brahmins reciting the four Vedas
and performing sacrifices spreads without ceasing everywhere.
Good paddy plants flourish there waving in the wind."
3481 pēr eyil cūḻ kaṭal teṉ ilaṅki
ceṟṟa pirāṉ vantu vīṟṟirunta
pēreyiṟkē pukku eṉ neñcam nāṭi
pērttu vara eṅkum kāṇamāṭṭēṉ
āri iṉi iṅku uṭiyam tōḻī?
eṉ neñcam kūva vallārum illi
āri iṉikkoṇṭu eṉ cātikkiṉṟatu?
eṉ neñcam kaṇṭatuvē kaṇṭēṉē (7)
3481. She says,
"O friend, the lord of Thirupereyil
who destroyed Lanka in the south
surrounded by large forts and the ocean
entered my heart
but I haven’t seen him for a long time now.
O my friend, who is there to help me?
No one is strong enough to call the god to my heart.
How can I find anyone to achieve anything?
My heart thinks whatever it knows is the right thing."
3482 kaṇṭatuvē koṇṭu ellārum kūṭi
kārk kaṭal vaṇṇaṉōṭu eṉ tiṟattuk
koṇṭu alar tūṟṟiṟṟu atu mutalāk
koṇṭa eṉ kātal urikkil tōḻī
maṇ tiṇi ñālamum ēḻ kaṭalum
nīḷ vicumpum kaḻiyap peritāl
teṇ tiri cūḻntu avaṉ vīṟṟirunta
teṉ tiruppēreyil cērvaṉ ceṉṟē (8)
3482. She says,
"O friend, when people see me,
they get together and gossip about the love I have
for the dark ocean-colored lord.
Why should I worry about their gossip?
My love for him is larger than this world filled with sand
and the seven oceans and the wide sky.
I will go to southern Thirupereyil
surrounded by the ocean with its clear waves."
3483 cērvaṉ ceṉṟu eṉṉuṭit tōḻimīrkāḷ
aṉṉiyarkāḷ eṉṉit tēṟṟa vēṇṭā
nīrkaḷ urikkiṉṟatu eṉ itaṟku?
neñcum niṟivum eṉakku iṅku illi
kārvaṇṇaṉ kārk kaṭal ñālam uṇṭa
kaṇṇa pirāṉ vantu vīṟṟirunta
ēr vaḷa oṇ kaḻaṉip paḻaṉa
teṉ tiruppēreyil mānakarē. (9)
3483. She says,
"O friends, mothers, I will go there and stay.
Don’t convince me to stay here.
Why do you want to stop me?
My heart and my chastity don’t want to stay here.
He is the dark ocean-colored lord Kaṇṇan
and he swallowed the world surrounded by the dark ocean.
He stays in wonderful southern Thirupereyil
filled with flourishing paddy fields plowed by farmers."
3484 nakaramum nāṭum piṟavum tērvēṉ
nāṇ eṉakku illi eṉ tōḻimīrkāḷ
cikara maṇi neṭu māṭam nīṭu
teṉ tiruppēreyil vīṟṟirunta
makara neṭuṅkuḻik kātaṉ māyaṉ
nūṟṟuvari aṉṟu maṅka nūṟṟa
nikar il mukilvaṇṇaṉ nēmiyāṉ eṉ
neñcam kavarntu eṉi ūḻiyāṉē? (10)
3484. She says,
" O friends, I will go to all towns and lands to find him,
I am not ashamed at all.
He is the god Makara Neḍum Kuzhi Kaadar and the Maayan.
He destroyed the Kauravas, the enemies of the Pandavas.
He has the color of a cloud and carries a matchless discus.
He attracted my heart.
He is the god on the earth for many eons."
3485 ūḻitōṟu ūḻi uruvum pērum
ceykiyum vēṟavaṉ viyam kākkum
āḻi nīr vaṇṇaṉi accutaṉi
aṇi kurukūrc caṭakōpaṉ coṉṉa
kēḻ il antāti ōr āyirattuḷ
ivi tiruppēreyil mēya pattum
āḻi aṅkiyaṉi ētta vallār
avar aṭimittiṟattu āḻiyārē. (11)
3485. Sadagopan of beautiful Thirukuruhur
composed a thousand anthadi pasurams on Achudan,
the ocean-colored god.
The lord protects the earth and has many names,
forms and deeds in every eon.
If devotees learn these ten poems
that prise the god of Thiruperelyil
who holds a discus in his hand
they will become his slaves.
------------
tiruvāymoḻi eḻām pattu (nāṉkām tirumoḻi, 3486-3496)
emperumāṉatu veṟṟic ceyalkaḷip pēcutal
3486 āḻi eḻa caṅkum villum eḻa tici
vāḻi eḻa taṇṭum vāḷum eḻa aṇṭam
mōḻi eḻa muṭi pātam eḻa appaṉ
ūḻi eḻa ulakam koṇṭavāṟē (1)
3486. When he took the form of Thirivikraman
to measure the world,
his discus, his conch, his bow,his club and sword
all appeared and everyone prised him everywhere.
Our father’s head touched the sky
and his feet touched the earth.
This is the way the world and the eons appeared.
3487 āṟu malikku etirntu ōṭum oli aravu
ūṟu culāy mali tēykkum oli kaṭal
māṟu cuḻaṉṟu aḻikkiṉṟa oli appaṉ
cāṟupaṭa amutam koṇṭa nāṉṟē (2)
3487. At the time when our father
churned the milky ocean to take the nectar,
the sound of many rivers that are born in the mountins
and fall down to the earth
and the sound of the churning of the ocean with Manthara mountin
and the loud sound of the snake Vasuki, all spread everywhere.
3488 nāṉṟila ēḻ maṇṇum tāṉattavē piṉṉum
nāṉṟila ēḻ mali tāṉattavē piṉṉum
nāṉṟila ēḻ kaṭal tāṉattavē appaṉ
ūṉṟi iṭantu eyiṟṟil koṇṭa nāḷē (3)
3488. At the time our father
took the form of a boar and split open the earth,
the seven worlds stayed where they were without moving.
The seven mountins stayed without shaking.
The seven oceans did not rise and were still.
3489 nāḷum eḻa nilam nīrum eḻa viṇṇum
kōḷum eḻa eri kālum eḻa mali
tāḷum eḻa cuṭar tāṉum eḻa appaṉ
ūḷi eḻa ulakam uṇṭa ūṇē (4)
3489. When the world was ending
our father swallowed the seven worlds,
the oceans, the lands, the sky, the stars,
fire, mountins, wind, moon and sun
